## PLAN FY2023

Pilbara Minerals is guided by its Operate, Grow, Diversify strategy, and is focused on ensuring sustainability is embedded throughout the entire business.

Through the execution of this strategy, Pilbara Minerals has emerged as a leading player in the world's rapidly growing lithium raw materials and downstream chemicals supply chain.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## OPERATE

•

- Safely mine, process and sell spodumene and tantalite concentrates

## GROW

- Continue to expand the production capacity of the Pilgangoora Project ( the Project ) to match market demand
- Increase the Project's JORC  resource and reserve, through further exploration and development

## DIVERSIFY

- Leverage  the  size  and  quality  of  the  Project to  create  strategic  growth  opportunities  by establishing  deeper  links  within  the  global lithium  raw  materials  and  chemicals  supply chain

Making the world a better place by enabling the global energy transformation.

Through conversations with our people, the Pilbara Purpose was born. Our purpose is the reason we do what we do. It is the foundation of our being and what drives and inspires us in our work every day. Our purpose is underpinned by 'What We Stand For'. Six commitments that are at the heart of our culture and how we work together to achieve our purpose.

## FROM THE CHAIR MESSAGE

Dear Stakeholder

I  am  pleased  to  present  Pilbara  Minerals'  Annual  and Sustainability  Report  for  the  2022  Financial  Year  and  to recap  what  has  been  an  excellent  year  for  the  Company, characterised by:

- A substantial increase in revenue to $1.2 billion ( B ), underpinning a net profit after tax of $561.8M.
- A significant 28% increase in spodumene shipments from FY2021.
- Excellent progress in respect of sustainability initiatives, in particular to support  our aim  of targeting net zero in the decade commencing 2040.
- Final  investment  decision  ( FID )  to  proceed  with the  P680  Expansion  Project  to  increase  annual production  at  the  Pilgan  Plant  by  100,000  dmt  to deliver  production  capacity  of  640-680,000  dry metric tonnes per annum ( dmtpa ) for the combined Pilgangoora Project.
- Completion  of  the  formal  Joint  Venture  ( JV )  with POSCO  for  a  downstream  project  to  construct  a 43,000  tonnes  per  annum  ( tpa )  lithium  hydroxide chemical facility in South Korea.

•

- Launch  of  the  Battery  Material  Exchange  ( BMX ), a new digital sales platform for spodumene concentrate, enabling price discovery in the lithium raw materials market.
- Progression of our Mid-stream Project with partner  Calix  Limited  Australia  ( Calix )  to  develop a  demonstration  plant  to  produce  value-added lithium salt (lithium phosphate), which if successful, will  deliver  a  less  carbon  intensive  product  for  the battery materials industry.

For personal use only

- Continued improvement in our safety performance as measured by both leading and lagging indicators.

The  above  achievements  would  not  have  been  possible without  a  dedicated  and  efficient  team  of  people,  whom have been with us over the journey. Most of these employees endured difficult operating and market conditions in the past and it is essential to acknowledge their contributions.

The  turnaround  in  the  lithium  market  has  been  by  any measure, very significant with exceptionally strong demand from  our  customer  base  and  record  pricing  outcomes reflecting a shortage of lithium raw materials globally.

The increase in demand is being driven by the growing use of lithium-ion batteries to drive decarbonisation, with the takeup of electric vehicles (EV) continuing to accelerate - not just in China - but worldwide.

Our ability to fully capitalise on this increasing demand was, I  believe,  due  in  part  to  a  series  of  far-sighted  commercial decisions made by the Company over the past few years.

An  integral  part  of  this  was  maintaining  our  working environment and culture, focused on retaining our people and making Pilbara Minerals a great place to work, in addition to, the implementation of a moderated production strategy during  the  market  downturn,  the  completion  of  plant improvement projects and acquiring the Ngungaju Plant.

Our  commitment to  advancing  our  growth  strategy,  even during the tough times, has been proven - positioning us now for long-term growth and success.

In recognising these achievements, I would like to re affirm again  the  outstanding  contributions  of  our  people  and  in doing so, draw particular attention to the leadership and vision of  Ken  Brinsden,  who  has  been  the  Company's  Managing Director  and  CEO  for  the  past  seven  years  and  formally stepped down on 30 July 2022.

Ken has been an exceptional leader and remains one of the global lithium industry's greatest advocates and I would like to sincerely thank Ken for his tireless service to Pilbara Minerals.

Ken's  successor  -  Dale  Henderson  -  is  well  equipped  to continue this legacy. Dale has been Chief Operating Officer at Pilbara Minerals for the past five years, knows the Company and its operations well, and has an intimate understanding of the global lithium industry.

Throughout the year, the Company has articulated a clear vision  for  shareholders  -  underpinned  by  our  strategy  to 'Operate', 'Grow' and 'Diversify'.

Our  achievements  in  each  of  these  areas  are  detailed within this report. However, I would like to acknowledge the Company's significant operational achievements in the face of the significant challenges arising from COVID-19 and the labour shortages in Western Australia.

The Pilbara Minerals team has done a fantastic job in building our  business,  while  continuing  to  maintain  the  highest standards of safety.

We have also maintained a strong focus on people, continuing to  build  a  positive  safety  culture  and  a  safe  and  respectful workplace,  as  well  as  fostering  our  close  and  collaborative relationship with the area's Traditional Owners, the Nyamal People, and other stakeholders.

In conclusion, I  would  like  to  thank  my  fellow  Board members for their strong counsel throughout the year and acknowledge  the  exceptional  efforts  of  Pilbara  Minerals' senior management.

I would also like to thank all our shareholders and partners for their ongoing support.

Anthony (Tony) Kiernan AM

## MESSAGE

## FROM THE MANAGING DIRECTOR AND CEO

## Dear Stakeholder

The past financial year has undoubtedly been one of the most exciting in the Company's history, firmly cementing Pilbara Minerals' status as one of the leading providers of lithium raw materials globally with an exceptional growth trajectory still in front of us.

I would like to acknowledge  the contribution of  my predecessor, Ken Brinsden, who has overseen the Company's remarkable  transformation  from  junior  developer  to  an ASX-100 Company.

Capitalising on  a very strong market, Pilbara Minerals delivered a robust operational performance from our Pilgangoora Project in FY2022, with a strong increase in both lithium production and pricing contributing to an exceptional bottom-line result.

Average  received  prices  increased  from  US$850-$900/dmt (CIF China) early in the reporting period, to US$4,267/dmt (CIF China) in the June 2022 Quarter. This translated to record sales revenue of $1.2B and a maiden net annual profit after tax of $561.8M.

A  key  element  of  this  outstanding  performance  has  been the  successful  integration  of  the  Ngungaju  Plant  -  which we acquired in January 2021 - into the Pilgangoora Project. Following  the  staged  re-start,  the  Ngungaju  Plant  is  now on-track to achieve nameplate production capacity of 180200,000 dmtpa during the September 2022 Quarter. This will increase the Pilgangoora Project's total production capacity to between 540,000 and 580,000 dmtpa at a time when global appetite for lithium is accelerating.

The Company has committed to additional steps to further increase production to support our long-term growth strategy and extract additional value from our operations.

The  FID  was  made  for  the  next  incremental  expansion of  the  Pilgangoora  Project  which  is  targeted  to  lift  the nameplate  production  capacity  from  ~580,000  to  680,000 tpa of spodumene  concentrate ( P680 Project ) at an estimated  capital  cost  of  $103M.  This  FID  also  includes an  integrated  ore  sorting  and  crushing  facility  to  replace the existing contracted crushing facility with a larger

5 Mtpa company-owned crushing facility at a capital estimate of $194M. The new crushing and ore sorting facility is expected to support future expansions at Pilgangoora up to 1,000,000 tpa ( P1000 Project ).  With  the  P680 Project now well underway, this additional capacity is targeted to come online during the December Quarter 2023.

The  Company  also  formalised  its  Joint  Venture  ( JV )  with POSCO to jointly develop and operate a 43,000 tpa lithium hydroxide  monohydrate  ( LHM )  conversion  facility  in  South Korea. This supports our strategic objective of becoming a fully  integrated  lithium  chemicals  company and diversifies our product base, providing exposure to the rapidly growing South Korean battery materials market.

We  have  also  made  positive  progress  with  our  project partner, Calix, to investigate an alternative lithium chemicals product  (mid-stream)  to  produce  a  concentrated  lithium salt  at  the  Pilgangoora  Project  utilising  electric  calcination. A  Scoping  Study  has  supported  the  technical  viability  of constructing  a  demonstration-scale  chemicals  facility  at the  Pilgangoora  Project,  and  we  have  recently  agreed  key commercial terms with Calix for the formation of a formal JV. During the year, the Company was successful in securing a Australian Government grant of $20M to contribute towards the demonstration plant and further this important project. The grant is expected to be formalised this calendar year.

The  mid-stream  processing  path  being  studied  has  the potential to materially reduce scope 3 carbon emissions, by utilising renewable energy power and significantly reducing the volume of waste being transported overseas through the production of a higher concentrate intermediary product at the Pilgangoora Project. It is expected these properties will be highly valued by the emerging markets in Europe and North America.

I am also pleased to report that the BMX digital sales platform established  during  the  year  to  facilitate  online  auctions of unallocated spodumene  concentrate, has been an outstanding success, achieving exceptionally strong pricing outcomes and bringing new customers to the Company. It has also led to never seen before price discovery for spodumene concentrate in the global market.

This incredible progress, by the Company has been achieved against the backdrop of significant macroeconomic challenges,  most  notably  the  continued  impact  of  the COVID-19 pandemic. The fact that we have been able to make such significant progress is testament to the hard work and dedication of the entire Pilbara Minerals team - including our Board, employees, contractors and project partners - and I would like to sincerely thank them for their contributions and efforts.

We have also maintained an unwavering commitment to our Environmental, Social and Governance principles, which you can find further information on throughout this report.

Pilbara  Minerals  has  an  exciting  future  ahead  as  a  major participant  in  the  lithium-ion  supply  chain  supporting  the global  energy  transition.  A  transition  that  will  define  the worldwide economy over the coming decades.

Dale Henderson Managing Director and CEO

## AND PROJECT OVERVIEW COMPANY

$6.82B MARKET CAPITALISATION 6

$874.2M CASH AT BANK AS AT 30 JUNE 20227

TIER 1 ASSET WITH STRONG GROWTH OPPORTUNITIES

ASX100 LISTED COMPANY

Pilbara Minerals is a leading ASX-listed lithium company, with 100% ownership of the Pilgangoora Operation and Project ( the Project ) - the largest independent hard-rock lithium operation in the world, with an estimated mine life of 26+ years.

The  Project  is  located  120km  from  Port  Hedland  in Western Australia's resource-rich Pilbara region on Nyamal and Kariyarra traditional lands.

Located in one of the world's premier mining jurisdictions, the Project has access to first-class infrastructure, including  road  and  port,  and  is  underpinned  by  strong environmental and safety regulations and social standards.

The Project has two processing plants, the Pilgan Plant which produces both spodumene and tantalite concentrates, and the  Ngungaju  Plant,  which  only  produces  a  spodumene concentrate.

## GROWTH AND PERFORMANCE ECONOMIC

FY2022 saw strong production and pricing underpinned by increasing global demand for lithium, primarily driven by the transport sector and the electrification of vehicles.

The  Pilgangoora  Project  produced  377,902  dry  metric tonnes  ( dmt )  and  shipped  361,035  dmt  of  spodumene concentrate  in  FY2022,  at  an  average  realised  price  of US$2,382/dmt (CIF China) to generate a substantial gross margin from operations for FY2022 of $853.5M10.

The Company introduced its digital sales platform BMX during  the  year,  which  achieved  outstanding  results across six auctions (which included one pre-auction sale), securing a maximum price of US$6,350/dmt SC5.5 basis (FOB Port Hedland) in the open market which equates to US$7,017/dmt on an SC6.0 equivalent basis (CIF China) after adjusting for lithia content and freight costs.

Pilbara  Minerals'  strong  balance  sheet  included  a  cash balance  of  $874.2M  at  the  end  of  the  reporting  period (inclusive  of  $282.4M  of  irrevocable  letters  of  credit  for shipments completed up to 30 June 2022), which ensures the  Company is well  placed  to  invest  in  further  growth opportunities to capitalise on the surging lithium market. The  Company's  growth  outlook  is  supported  by  an expansion and diversification pathway that underpins its long-term strategy to unlock the full value of the Project and  become  fully  integrated  within  the  lithium  raw materials and chemicals value chain.

In June 2022, Pilbara Minerals' Board approved the capital investment for the P680 Project to deliver a 100,000 tpa increase  in  average  annual  spodumene  concentrate production capacity from the Pilgan Plant. This is expected to  increase  the  total  annual  production  run  rate  at  the combined Pilgangoora Project to approximately 640,000680,000 dmt11 from the December Quarter 2023.

## MARKET

## UPDATE

FY2022  marked  an  incredible  growth  chapter  for  the lithium  raw  materials  industry,  with  both  demand  and pricing reaching unprecedented levels.

The  continued  rapid  growth  was  underpinned  by  the expansion of the global lithium-ion battery manufacturing industry to feed the electrification of the transport sector. This  growth is  the  result  of  the  concerted  shift  towards decarbonisation  and  net  zero  targets  being  set  by  the private sector and governments around the globe.

During FY2022, Pilbara Minerals achieved strong pricing outcomes including prices of US$6,586/dmt and  US$7017/dmt  on  an  SC6.0  equivalent  basis  (CIF China) for cargoes of spodumene  concentrate  sold in  May  and  June  on  the  BMX  digital  sales  platform. Subsequent to the end of the financial year, selling prices continued to remain strong with prices of US$6,841/dmt and  US$7,012/dmt  SC6.0  equivalent  basis  (CIF  China) achieved  on  the  BMX  platform  during  July  and  August 2022.

This was mirrored in the broader lithium market, where continued market strength saw spodumene prices increase  from  US$868/dmt  (CIF  China)  in  July  2021, to US$5,462/dmt  SC6.0  equivalent basis (CIF China) as  reported  by  external  agencies12  by  the  end  of  the reporting period.

Pricing  for  FY2023  is  expected  to  remain  strong,  with growing  evidence  that  the  global  lithium  deficit  will continue to widen as demand outstrips supply.

A  recent  report  published  by  the  International  Energy Agency ( IEA ) concluded that global battery and minerals supply chains need to expand tenfold to meet projected critical minerals needs by 2030.

The IEA expects demand for EV batteries to increase from around  340  Gigawatt  hours  ( GWh )  today  to  over  3,500 GWh  by  2030,  with  the  industry  projected  to  need  50 more lithium mines by 2030 to meet global net carbon emissions goals.

Highly  regarded  industry  analyst,  Benchmark  Mineral Intelligence, has recently predicted that the supply deficit will reach ~1.8 Mt of lithium carbonate equivalent by 2040, equivalent to ~18 new lithium operations the size of the Project.

Further supporting evidence of this growth can be found in the European Union's Joint Research Centre forecasts that  show  demand  for  lithium  is  likely  to  be  almost  60 times higher than current consumption by 2050.

Recent  activity including customer  supply  enquiries during FY2022 support these findings and highlight the difficulties  being  experienced  by  the  global  transport industry in securing  lithium  offtake, as well as the strategic  value  of  having  uncontracted  tonnes  in  the current market environment.

Lithium deficit is expected to grow

## ADDING VALUE THROUGH INNOVATION

Pilbara  Minerals  progressed  two  innovative  diversification  projects  during  the  year  which  aim  to  capture  additional value along the lithium supply chain.

In March 2022, Pilbara Minerals completed a scoping study with Calix to generate a higher value and lower carbon energy intensive lithium product for the battery materials industry, this new product is referred to as a 'mid-stream' product. The aim of the Mid-stream Project development is to deliver a superior 'value-added' lithium salt that outperforms across the key metrics that include cost, quality, carbon energy intensity and waste.

Additionally, Pilbara Minerals finalised its participation in an incorporated joint venture with POSCO in April 2022 to develop and operate a 43,000 tpa LHM Conversion Facility in South Korea.

Both the Mid-stream Project and POSCO joint venture strategically position Pilbara Minerals as an integrated lithium raw materials and chemical supplier, thus enabling the Company to extract additional value from the Project and therefore the opportunity to deliver enhanced economic returns per lithium unit to its shareholders in the years ahead.

2022 ANNUAL REPORT

Pilbara Minerals

For personal use only

<!-- image -->

Located  120  km  from  Port  Hedland  in  Western  Australia's  Pilbara  region, Pilbara Minerals' 100%-owned Pilgangoora Operation and Project ( the Project ) is positioned close to key infrastructure (including road and port) and export markets.

The  Project  is  located  on  Wallareenya  Station,  which  covers  Nyamal  and Kariyarra country.

The  Project  comprises  34  granted  tenements  spanning  1086.42  square kilometres  ( km2 ).  It  operates  in  accordance  with  Native  Title  Agreements, Pastoral  Leases  and  Project  Infrastructure  Agreements,  as  well  as  relevant national and state legal and other regulatory requirements. Additional detail about the Project area is provided in the Sustainability section of this report.

The  broader  region  hosts  multiple  mining  and  exploration  leases  and operations that co-exist with pastoral, conservation and tourism sectors.

<!-- image -->

## EXPLORATION AND GEOLOGY

The  Project  is  strategically  positioned  within  Western  Australia's  resourcerich  Pilbara  region.  The  Company  has  a  consolidated  tenure  within  the Pilgangoora  pegmatite  district  within  the  Strelley,  Tabba  Tabba,  Wodgina, and Mount Francisco districts that together form part of one of the largest rare-metal pegmatite provinces in the world. Most of the regional tenements are greenfields opportunities that are prospective for a range of commodities including rare-metals, gold and base metals.

During  the  year,  Pilbara  Minerals  completed  a  strategic  exploration  and resource  development  drilling  program  adjacent  to  the  historical  Altura tenement  boundary.  The  drilling  campaign  led  to  the  discovery  of  new pegmatite  domains,  and  together  with  the  integration  of  the  existing Ngungaju Mineral Resource and Ore Reserve resulted in substantial increases to the Project's Mineral Resource and Ore Reserve. Details of the exploration program and subsequent Resource and Reserve statements were provided in the Company's ASX announcements dated 6 September 2021 and 6 October 2021. A significant resource development drilling is scheduled to commence in the December 2022 Quarter.

The  Company  also  completed  a  co-funded  State  Government  Exploration Incentive  Scheme drilling  program  to  test  the  depth  extent  of  the  Pilgangoora rare-metal  pegmatite  system  with  results  expected  during  the  December  2022 Quarter.

## OPERATIONS

## OVERVIEW

FY2022 saw a strong ramp up in mining and processing capacity at the Project with the duel aims of increasing waste removal to uncover additional ore supply and ensuring maximum plant throughput was achieved. The Company continued the restart, commissioning and ramp up of the Ngungaju Plant with key milestones including the delivery of first ore from the coarse production circuit early in October 2021, and first ore from the fines circuit in early June 2022.

## MINING AND PROCESSING

Mining  activity  progressively  scaled  up  during the second half of FY2022 in response to strengthening  market  conditions  and  increased customer demand.

Total  production  for  FY2022  comprised  377,902 dmt of spodumene concentrate (FY2021: 281,098 dmt), whilst total material mined was 21.6M wet metric tonnes ( wmt ) (FY2021: 6.7M wmt).

Increased waste movements during FY2022 supported mine development activities and provided access to additional  ore.  It  is  expected that waste movements will continue to be higher over  the  next  12-24  months  as  the  Company accesses more ore for production growth in the Central Pit Stage 2 and 3, South and Eastern Pit development areas.

Spodumene  concentrate  production  from  the Pilgan  Plant  increased  to  321,221  dmt  (FY2021: 281,098 dmt). Production volumes were impacted by workforce shortfalls for employees and contractors  due  to  the  COVID-19  pandemic  as well  as  tight  labour  market  conditions.  Lithia recoveries  were  lower  than  planned  impacted by  less-than-optimal  ore  feed  to  the  plant  due to  mining  constraints,  as  well  as  work  required to  tie-in  the  plant  improvement  works  earlier  in the year. Following the successful commissioning of  the  Pilgan  Plant  Improvement  Project  in  the December  2021  Quarter, installed production capacity at the Pilgan Plant  increased  from 330,000 tpa to ~360,000-380,000 tpa of spodumene concentrate. The Pilgan Plant performed to expectations following completion and commissioning of the improvement works.

Full year unit operating cost13 for the Pilgan Plant (FOB  Port  Hedland  and  excluding  freight  and royalties)  was  $555/dmt  (FY2021:  $431/dmt).  This higher  cost  was  a  result  of  manning  shortfalls which translated to reduced mined volumes and less-than-optimal  ore  feed  to  the  Pilgan  Plant impacting production and recoveries, as well as unplanned/extended shutdowns. Inflationary pressures  also  impacted  operating  costs  during the second half of FY2022 including an increase in  diesel  fuel  prices,  ocean  freight,  reagent  and labour costs.

Including freight and  royalty costs, the unit operating cost for the year was $844/dmt (FY2021: $519/dmt) with significantly higher selling prices translating to substantially higher royalty costs.

The staged restart of the Ngungaju Plant commenced  following  Pilbara  Minerals'  Board approval in June 2021. During this commissioning and ramp-up phase, production of 56,681 dmt of spodumene concentrate was achieved from the Ngungaju Plant during FY2022 (FY2021: nil).

## FY2022 total production and shipments

## IMPROVEMENT WORKS

In  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  completed  the  Pilgan  Plant Improvement  Project. The aim of the Pilgan Plant Improvement Project was to unlock an additional 10-15% (~30-50,000  tpa)  of  production  capacity  by  increasing plant  operating  time  and  throughput,  lowering  final product moisture and managing product recovery performance.  The  works  included  the  installation  of  an additional filter press to improve the performance of the fines concentrate circuit.

Following  the  successful  commissioning  of  the  Pilgan Plant Improvement Project, installed production capacity has  now  been  re-rated  from  330,000  tpa  to  360,000380,000 tpa of spodumene concentrate.

Ngungaju Plant improvement initiatives  are  continuing with further scoping being undertaken to increase throughput and plant recovery rates. These improvements are  aimed  at  improving  the  floatation  circuit  and  are planned to be completed progressively during FY2023.

For personal use only

<!-- image -->

## Ngungaju Plant crew

Mining  optimisation  will  be  a  key  focus  in  FY2023  with planned  investment  in  production  monitoring  systems, enhanced  communications  and  operational  excellence projects. These projects will focus on mining  fleet, operating efficiencies and reporting platforms.

## GROWTH PROJECTS

During the year, Pilbara Minerals' Board approved a FID to  expand  production capacity at the Pilgan Plant by a further 100,000 dmt per annum to increase the Project's combined  annual  nameplate  production  capacity  from ~540,000 - 580,000 dmt to between ~640,000 - 680,000 dmt  per annum  of spodumene  concentrate (P680 Project).  The  P680  Project  has  $50M  of  pre-investment capital towards  future expansions  up  to 1 Mtpa  of spodumene concentrate production (P1000 Project).

Key activities for the P680 Project include:

- Construction  of  primary  rejection  heavy  media separation circuit ( Primary Rejection ) enabling rejection of low-grade waste material and providing an additional 100,000 tpa of spodumene concentrate  production  capacity  at  an  estimated capital cost of $103.0M. Expansion pathway to 1Mtpa
- Construction  of  an  integrated  crushing  and  ore sorting facility capable of processing up to 5 Mtpa of  ore  throughput and supporting further process improvements and concentrate quality, at an estimated capital cost of $194.5M.

Commissioning of the Primary Rejection circuit is expected  to  commence  from  September  Quarter  2023, with  the  additional  production  capacity  to  follow  three months later.

This expansion increases capacity, while also supporting the Company's decarbonisation strategy by rejecting more waste at the front end of the processing circuit, reducing overall energy intensity and carbon emissions per spodumene concentrate tonne.

In  line  with  Pilbara  Minerals'  growth  strategy,  scoping activities are continuing for the P1000 Project to increase spodumene concentrate for the combined Project up to ~1 Mtpa 14 .

14 Refer ASX announcement 3 August 2022

Accelerated growth to capture improved demand conditions and increase revenue

## OFFTAKE AGREEMENTS

## PILGAN PLANT

Following  completion  of  the  Pilgan  Plant  Improvement Project and ramp up, the annualised nameplate spodumene  concentrate  production  capacity at the Pilgan  Plant  was  re-rated  from  approximately  ~330,000 dmt to 360-380,000 dmt.

This  production  is  allocated  under  long-term  offtake agreements with tier one customers including:

- Ganfeng Lithium -  China's  largest  fully  integrated lithium chemical converter.
- General Lithium - major producer of battery grade lithium chemicals.
- Great Wall Motor Company - one of China's largest car and EV manufacturers.
- Yibin Tianyi -an emerging lithium hydroxide producer in China with CATL as its major shareholder.
- POSCO  -  the  world's  fifth  largest  steel  producer, currently  building  out  a  full  value  chain  for  raw materials for the lithium-ion battery.

## NGUNGAJU PLANT

Pilbara Minerals commenced a staged recommissioning of the Ngungaju Plant during FY2022, with first concentrate produced  from  the  coarse  ore  circuit  in  October  2021. Production from the Ngungaju Plant is currently largely sold into the spot market via Pilbara Minerals' BMX digital sales  platform,  but  eventually  will  provide  supply  to  the Company's  43,000  tpa  LHM  lithium  hydroxide  plant  in South  Korea  jointly  owned  with  POSCO.  This  facility, which is expected to start early-stage commissioning in the  second  half  of  2023  calendar  year  with  ramp  up  in 2024 calendar year, will be supplied with up to 315,000 tpa of  spodumene  concentrates  under  a  long-term  offtake agreement sourced from both the Ngungaju Plant and the Pilgan Plant following completion of the P680 Project.

## PROJECT EXPANSION

On 29 June 2022,  Pilbara  Minerals'  Board  approved  the capital  investment  for  the  'P680  Project'  to  deliver  a 100,000 tpa increase in the Pilgan Plant's average annual spodumene concentrate production capacity.

Production from the P680 Project will provide additional supply to support the chemical conversion facility currently  under  development  in  South  Korea  in  joint venture  with  POSCO  (refer  ASX  announcement  11  April 2022).

## SALES AND SHIPPING

## SPODUMENE

During  FY2022,  34  cargoes  of  spodumene  concentrate were  shipped  via  15  vessels,  with  China  the  primary destination for these cargoes. Of these shipments, three cargoes  were  delivered  to  South  Korea  to  support  the POSCO demonstration plant.

Pilbara Minerals shipped a total of 361,035 dmt  of spodumene concentrate in FY2022, up from 281,440 dmt in FY2021 (sales growth of 28% YoY).

On  20  June  2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  achieved  its  highest price in FY2022 for spodumene concentrate via its BMX sales  platform,  reaching  an  equivalent  SC6.0  equivalent basis (CIF China) price of US$7,017/dmt.

Revenue  from  spodumene  sales  for  the  year  totalled $1.2B.

## TANTALITE

In  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  sold  76,367  lbs  of  primary tantalite concentrate to Global Advanced  Metals, a vertically  integrated  provider  of  tantalum  metallurgical products  and  tantalum  powders  for  high-performance capacitators, with a total value of $5.4M.

2022 ANNUAL REPORT

Pilbara Minerals

## BMX - BATTERY MATERIAL EXCHANGE

Late in FY2021, the Company introduced a digital trading and sales platform, the BMX platform, to facilitate the sale of its uncommitted spodumene concentrate production on the spot market.

The platform proved to be an extremely successful sales avenue for the Company with strong interest shown in all six cargo auctions.

Six separate cargoes of spodumene concentrate were auctioned during FY2022, totalling approximately 43,000 dmt.

The June 2022, cargo achieved a record offer of US$6,350/dmt SC5.5 basis (FOB Port Hedland) which equated to an approximate price of US$7,017/dmt on a SC6.0 equivalent basis (CIF China), after adjusting for lithia content on a pro rata basis and freight costs.

The Company anticipates strong pricing through the BMX platform to likely continue in FY2023 based on the robust demand for battery raw materials currently being experienced across the global lithium-ion supply chain.

## JOINT VENTURE PROJECTS

## POSCO DOWNSTREAM PROJECT

Pilbara  Minerals'  longstanding  relationship  with  South Korean  conglomerate  POSCO  went  from  strength-to strength in FY2022, with the two companies finalising a JV in April 2022 (refer ASX announcement dated 11 April 2022) for  the  development  of  a  lithium  hydroxide  conversion facility in Gwangyang, South Korea.

The  JV  places  Pilbara  Minerals  and  POSCO  in  a  strong position to participate in the chemical conversion industry as one of the few near-term lithium chemicals producers outside of China with underwritten raw materials supply from  the  Project.  The  JV  supports  Pilbara  Minerals' strategic objective of becoming a fully integrated lithium raw  materials  company  and  diversifies  the  Company's reach  in  the  lithium  industry  beyond  spodumene  and merchant markets.

Based on the studies and engineering work undertaken since  October  2021,  the  capital  development  costs  for the conversion facility (100%) are estimated by POSCO at US$670-720M 16  (excluding  any  contingency  allowance). After allowing for initial working capital and preproduction financing costs, the total funding requirement for the JV is expected to be approximately US$750-800M.

Pilbara Minerals will initially own an 18% interest in the JV (largely funded from a $79.6M Convertible Bond provided by POSCO) with the right to increase its interest to 30% at  a  future  point  in  time  and  at  the  same  subscription price.  As  part  of  the  arrangement,  Pilbara  Minerals  will supply  315,000  tpa  of  spodumene  concentrate  from the  Project  on  commercial  terms  to  the  jointly  owned conversion  facility.  This  will  be  sourced  from  Pilbara Minerals'  production  capacity  at  the  Project  from  both the Ngungaju Plant and the Pilgan Plant.

Commissioning of  the  conversion  facility  is  expected  in the  second  half  of  2023  calendar  year  with  ramp  up  in 2024 calendar year.

16USD costs are estimated at a USD:KRW exchange rate of 1200

## MID-STREAM PROJECT

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals made significant progress with its  partner  Calix,  a  Sydney-based  technology  company, to  pursue a mid-stream project. This work supports the development of a demonstration-scale chemicals facility at the Project aimed at developing, and commercialising a  new,  innovative  refining  process  to  produce  a  valueadded, lower carbon energy intensive, lithium salt product.

If successful, the mid-stream product will help to decarbonise the lithium chemicals supply chain.

The  Mid-stream  Project  will  exclusively  utilise Calix's innovative  and  patented  electric  calciner  technology. Being  an  electric  process,  this  will  enable  the  use  of renewable power sources.

In March FY2022, Pilbara Minerals and Calix completed a scoping study that provided preliminary support for the technical viability of the demonstration plant (refer ASX announcement 31 March 2022).

In  June  2022,  and  following  the  award  of  a  $20M  grant from the Australian Government, Calix and Pilbara Minerals  entered  into  a  binding  MoU  to  record  their agreement on key commercial terms for a JV supporting the  development  of  the  Mid-stream  Project  (refer  ASX announcement dated 1 June 2022). Upon formation of the JV, participating interests will be 55% Pilbara Minerals and 45% Calix with each party funding their agreed share of operating and capital costs and licensing their respective technology into the JV.

The parties are aiming to finalise a Formal JV Agreement with a FID expected in early 2023.

The $20M funding grant from the Australian Government remains conditional on the parties entering into a customary funding agreement, which is expected to be finalised in the December 2022 Quarter.

## CASE STUDY

## INNOVATION IN TECHNOLOGY

In  May  2022,  Pilbara  Minerals,  and  Calix,  were  awarded  a  $20M  grant  from  the  Australian  Government  under  the Modern Manufacturing Initiative ( MMI ) - Manufacturing Translation Stream, supporting the further development and demonstration of the proposed Mid-stream Project.

The grant is a vote of endorsement for this project and will be used to support the development of a demonstration scale chemicals facility at the Project under the proposed joint venture between Pilbara Minerals and Calix.

The demonstration plant will exclusively utilise Calix's innovative calciner technology for lithium processing applications, with  the  aim  of  producing  lithium  salts  for  global  distribution  via  the  innovative  mid-steam  'value  added'  refining process.

The MMI grant will assist in proving up the technology proposed under the Mid-stream Project and has the potential to deliver significant benefits across the industry including:

Pilbara Minerals 2022 ANNUAL REPORT For personal use only

- Increased downstream 'value-add' realisation within the Australian economy and locally in the Pilbara region.
- Significant  sustainability  benefits  across  the  lithium  industry  via  electrification  of  the  'value-added'  process (including spodumene calcining), thereby enabling a decarbonisation pathway, as well as rationalisation of the carbon footprint via reduced waste movements across transport and logistics supply chains from a near zerowaste final product.
- Maximising hard-rock lithium resources via improved lithium recovery due to an efficient process of spodumene concentrates conversion.

A funding agreement with the Australian Government is expected to be finalised in the December 2022 Quarter.

## FINANCE

Pilbara  Minerals  responded  to  significantly  improved market demand conditions for battery raw materials over the  course  of  the  year  by  delivering  record  production, shipping  and  pricing  outcomes  despite  a  backdrop of  challenging  business  conditions.  In  FY2022,  Pilbara Minerals  generated  revenue  of  $1.2B,  an  EBITDA17  of $814.5M and a net profit after tax of $561.8M. The Company finished FY2022 with a strong cash balance of $874.2M, inclusive of $282.4M of irrevocable bank letters of credit for shipments completed prior to the end of the reporting period.

During  the  year,  the  Company  finalised  an  agreement with its existing senior lenders to increase the Syndicated Finance Facility by US$20M to US$130M and the Working Capital  Facility  by  US$10M  to  US$25M  to  support  the restart  of  the  Ngungaju  Plant  in  December  2021.  The terms and conditions of the expanded Syndicated Finance Facility remained substantially the same as those executed  under  the  original  debt  facility  in  July  2020. The Working Capital Facility remains undrawn at 30 June 2022.

17To provide additional insight into the business, the Company uses unaudited non-IFRS measures such as 'gross margin from operations', 'EBITDA', 'underlying profit before tax and acquisition costs', 'net finance costs excluding deferred consideration' and 'net cash/debt'. Reconciliations of these amounts to IFRS measures are provided in the 'FY22 Annual Financial Report' lodged on the ASX platform on 22 August 2022

The strong financial performance during FY2022 allowed the Company to realise the benefit of prior year unrecognised  tax  losses  of  $66.3M  (tax  effected;  net  of deferred  tax  balances),  delivering  an  effective  tax  rate for  the  year  of  22.5%.  The  Company  also  recognised  a non-cash  deferred  consideration  fair  value  movement expense of $37.2M following the early settlement of the outstanding deferred consideration due to the Altura loan noteholders as part of the acquisition of Altura Lithium Operations Pty Ltd by way of shares. Prior to settlement, Pilbara Minerals executed a variation deed to reduce the number of shares to be paid in satisfaction of the deferred consideration  by  ~5%.  The  deferred  consideration  was settled  by  way  of  two  equal  tranches  of  32.67  M  shares each, on 17 August 2021 and 8 October 2021.

## FROM THE SUSTAINABILITY CHAIR MESSAGE

Dear Stakeholder

Pilbara Minerals embraces the important role it plays to make the world a better place by enabling a global energy transformation. At the core of Pilbara Minerals' purpose (why we do what we do) is to make the world a better place.

Throughout this year of significant growth, Pilbara Minerals has  maintained  its  focus  on  refreshing  and  integrating sustainability into day-to-day activities. As Pilbara Minerals grows  it  is  increasingly  important  that  the  Company identifies  opportunities  to  integrate  sustainability  not only through its operations, but in respect of its growth and  diversification  initiatives.  To  do  this,  the  Company has  been  updating  its  policies  and  processes  to  ensure sustainability  is  considered  when  making  operational changes and considering any investment decisions.

With  the  demand  for  lithium  raw  materials  increasing, and the world looking for answers to large-scale energy reduction,  Pilbara  Minerals  is  ensuring  it  continues  to keep sustainability at its core and 'walk the talk' towards global energy transition.

Throughout  FY2022  Pilbara  Minerals  has  maintained a  sharp  focus  on  advancing  the  Company's  net  zero pathway, integrating sustainability practices throughout the business, engaging employees, and making significant  progress  on  the  most  important  priorities. Some of the highlights you will see in this report include:

- Materiality assessment refresh.
- Climate change and emissions reduction.
- Traditional owner relationships and opportunities.
- Inclusion and wellbeing.
- Protecting human rights.

Building  on  the  materiality  assessment  refresh,  these initiatives  will  expand  in  FY2023  through  implementing internal  enablers  that  will  further  guide  the  business across  all  areas.  These  measures  will  include  setting key  sustainability  metrics  and  targets  for  priority  areas, and  building  them  into  routine  activities,  capturing sustainability related data to improve business decisions and  alignment  with  operational  frameworks  to  ensure the great work  achieved  in the business is readily accessible to interested stakeholders.

<!-- image -->

Sally-Anne Layman Chair, Sustainability Committee

<!-- image -->

## SUSTAINABILITY

Pilbara Minerals is enabling the global transition towards a clean energy future by mining and processing hard-rock lithium ore to produce a spodumene concentrate (lithium raw material) that is a critical raw material for the renewable energy supply chain.

Lithium raw material supply is crucial to manufacture batteries that power clean energy technologies, which are key to enabling the many decarbonisation targets set across the globe, to ensure the global temperature rise this century is limited to well below two degrees Celsius above pre-industrial levels.

Pilbara Minerals' Sustainability Policy and Strategy underpin the Company's approach to sustainability and support its integration into day-to-day business. The Sustainability Pillars which link to people, planet and profit, sit within Pilbara Minerals' Purpose and ensure focus on all areas of sustainability both internally and externally.

VISION

Make a positive contribution, minimise our impacts and leverage opportunities

## Sustainability achievements in FY2022

## SUSTAINABILITY GOVERNANCE

Pilbara Minerals recognises the power good governance can have, benefiting the Company, the people within it, as well as its shareholders and other key stakeholders.

Good  governance  is  key  to  realising  opportunities  and driving  sustainability  from  within  the  Company.  For Pilbara  Minerals,  its  about  accountability,  transparency and efficiency and fostering higher levels of stakeholder involvement. At Pilbara Minerals, the overall responsibility for  sustainability  lies  with  the  Board  and  is  overseen  by the Sustainability Committee.

The Sustainability Committee comprises three independent Non-executive Directors and meets quarterly  to  discuss  overarching  topics,  initiatives  and review metrics and track targets.

At  the  Project  level,  mining  and  processing  activities are governed by a robust regulatory environment and  comprehensive  internal  processes  that  focus  on resource efficiency (including water and land), regulatory compliance, stakeholder consultation and continual improvement to minimise impacts.

<!-- image -->

Sustainability Reporting Plan

## SUSTAINABILITY STRATEGY AND TIMELINE

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals continued to work through its Sustainability Reporting Plan to support the integration of sustainability into the business. Key initiatives completed included:

- Material issue refresh in consultation with stakeholders.
- Reporting against internal metrics and targets.
- Alignment with external reporting frameworks (Sustainable  Accounting  Standards  Board  ( SASB ), Taskforce  on  Climate-related  Financial  Disclosures ( TCFD ),  United  Nations  Sustainable  Development Goals ( UNSDGs ).

## FRAMEWORKS AND DISCLOSURE

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals made significant progress reporting against metrics, setting targets and aligning with  the  frameworks  identified  in  FY2021,  including SASB, TCFD and the UNSDGs. Full disclosure against SASB, under the category of 'Extractives and Minerals Processing, Metals and Mining Industry' is provided in the SASB summary table (Appendix).

A full multi-disciplinary disclosure review is currently being  undertaken  to  ensure  alignment  with  the lithium  industry  and  value  chain  requirements,  as well as stakeholder expectations.

## MATERIALITY ASSESSMENT REFRESH

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals completed a materiality assessment refresh that included a review of market trends, industry insights  and  key  stakeholder  views,  to  ensure  the  material  topics  outlined  would  be  relevant  to  the  Company  as  it continues to grow. The materiality assessment formed the foundation of the updated Sustainability Strategy which focuses on embedding the topics that matter most into the business. The assessment was undertaken by an external consultant to allow for greater objectivity.

## THE PROCESS

Pilbara Minerals' materiality methodology brought engagement to  the  forefront,  with  an  aim  to  integrate internal  and  external  stakeholders'  knowledge  into  the process.  To  allow  for  continuous  collaboration,  Pilbara Minerals  designed  a  materiality  methodology  which involved a strategic approach to stakeholder engagement. This was  deployed  to ensure all stakeholders were identified and suitably involved. Engagement took place between  March  and  July  2022  and  has  been  pivotal  to the  development  of  an  inclusive  Sustainability  Strategy that responds to stakeholder priorities and the sustainability risks most material to the Company.

## IDENTIFICATION

The FY2022 refresh consolidated the previous materiality assessment findings with a comprehensive internal and external stakeholder engagement process. A full desktop review also captured peer and industry, as well as market trends.  Views,  current  systems  and  trends  informed the  materiality  assessment  enabling  a  balanced  set  of sustainability materiality topics.

## PRIORITISATION

Material topics were prioritised according to significance and urgency. Stakeholder interest played a large role in prioritisation of topics and determination of significance. Urgency scores were given to material topics based on the severity  and  likelihood  of  key  themes  within  the  topics. All  material topics identified are of great significance to Pilbara  Minerals  however,  they  were  ranked  due  to  the necessity  of  providing  a  greater  focus  for  sustainability now.

## SUSTAINABLE DEVELOPMENT

## CASE STUDY GOALS

The UNSDGs work toward building a more sustainable future for all by addressing global challenges. The UNSDGs (2016-2030) build on the foundational Millennium Development  Goals  (2000-2015)  and  cover  17  priority  areas  for  governments worldwide  to  establish  national  frameworks.  These  priority  areas  are  a  great starting point for all governments, businesses and society to take action against goals that can help solve the planet's pressing issues.

For  companies  to  create  targets  to  progress  towards  this  better  future,  it  is important for them to understand where current (and potential future) impacts may be. The framework for developing action against the UNSDGs incorporated key areas such as the identification of business opportunities, risk management, strengthening stakeholder relationships and eventually making policy and regulatory amendments. Pilbara Minerals  thoroughly  assessed  internal  and  external  risks and opportunities and further strengthened this area as part of its materiality assessment refresh to progress its contribution to the UNSDGs.

## CLIMATE CHANGE

AND EMISSIONS REDUCTION

## Optimising operations to meet net zero target and support our role in powering a sustainable energy future.

Pilbara Minerals recognises the important role it plays in supporting global efforts to manage the increasing risks of climate change, in both the regions and communities where  it  operates.  The  Company  has  been  proactive  in its  response  to  this  global  challenge  by  maturing  and implementing a suite of initiatives to manage the physical risks, as well as identify the opportunities to drive down its impact on climate change. The Company has committed to  sustainable  consumption  and  production  and  will continue  to  investigate  ways  to  fast  track  its  emissions reduction pathway.

During FY2022, the Pilbara Minerals Sustainability Committee  and  Management  team  have  undertaken several initiatives throughout the Company including:

- Endorsement of Pilbara Minerals' net zero pathway.
- Incorporation of climate change  risks into the Company's Risk Register.
- Completion  of  a  life  cycle  assessment  ( LCA )  to identify  scope  3  emissions  to  the  point  of  lithium hydroxide production.
- Further maturity of TCFD disclosures.
- Embedding a sustainability framework within the  business  to  support  project  and  investment decision-making.

## INTERNATIONAL DEVELOPMENTS

In  its  FY2021  Annual  and  Sustainability  Report,  Pilbara Minerals identified that a pivotal moment had been  reached  in the fight against climate  change. Developments  in  FY2022  are  intensifying  this  sense  of urgency to act on climate change within this decade.

The  Glasgow  Climate  Pact  that  emerged  from  COP26 requires countries to strengthen their 2030 climate targets in 2022. Since COP26, the change in Federal government in  Australia  has  led  to  legislation  that  will  enshrine  into law a target to cut national GHG emissions by a minimum of 43% by 2030, and a reduction to net zero by 2050.

The year also saw two further instalments of the Intergovernmental  Panel  on  Climate  Change  (IPCC's) Sixth  Assessment  Report  (AR6).  In  February  2022,  the IPCC  warned  of  catastrophic  impacts  on  communities and  ecosystems  in  its  Working  Group  II  report  Climate Change 2022: Impacts, Adaptation and Vulnerability. The assessment in relation to Australasia found that climate trends and extreme events have already combined with exposure and vulnerabilities to cause major impacts for natural and human systems.

At  the  time  of  writing,  the  IPCC  Working  Group  III  had also  released  a  report:  Climate  Change  2022:  Mitigation of  Climate  Change,  which  found  that  there  was  only  a limited chance of limiting global warming to 1.5 C above pre-industrial levels.

These  developments  have  galvanised  Pilbara  Minerals' efforts  to  remain  aligned  with  the  Paris  Agreement's long-term temperature goals and continue its progress on the Company's decarbonisation pathway.

Pilbara Minerals

## RESPONDING TO CLIMATE CHANGE

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals continued to focus on the following capabilities in order to support its position on climate change:

- Climate-related risk management and TCFD alignment.
- Emissions tracking and reporting.
- Emissions reduction.
- Energy management.

To support this strategy, Pilbara Minerals actively strives to create and maintain a workplace culture where climate change and environmental impacts are prioritised, and where carbon reduction opportunities are identified and implemented, with the intent to deliver long-term benefits for future generations.

## CLIMATE-RELATED RISK MANAGEMENT

Pilbara Minerals is facing both transitional and physical risks from climate change. Over the course of the last 12 months these risks have routinely been assessed to identify the greatest impact to Pilbara Minerals' operations and incorporated into the Company's Risk Register with associated mitigative and reactive controls. Pilbara Minerals aims to mitigate these risks to protect its people, assets, operations and neighbours from the adverse impacts of climate change. This risk assessment and management process will be ongoing and performed with the support of various climate scenarios and selected climate-related impacts. The four hypotheses which have been assessed include:

## Climate Scenario Analysis Hypothesis

| Chronic Climate Change  •  Reduced precipitation and prolonged temperature increase in the Pilbara  region will impact the resilience of Pilbara Minerals' infrastructure to long-  term climatic changes.  Climate Extremes  •  Key  assets  will  experience  increased  frequency  and  severity  of  extreme  temperatures,  drought,  and  wet  extremes  impacting  Pilbara  Minerals'  production capabilities and contributing to worsening working conditions.  Market Transition  •  Increased  electrification  of  the  global  energy  storage  market  and  shifts  in global policy and regulatory landscape will drive increased demand for  lithium ore and compounds.  Carbon Pricing  •  Increased exposure to domestic/international carbon pricing mechanisms  will  increase  costs  of  production  and  impact  the  export  price  of  Pilbara  Minerals' end products.   |
|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

For personal use only

In FY2022, these risks were introduced into the Company's Risk Register, verified and risk controls were added. The Pilbara Minerals Audit and Risk Committee review and interrogate risks on a routine basis and provide a summary to the Board.

Pilbara Minerals is committed to understanding how both the physical impacts of climate change and the transition to low carbon operations might affect the business. Pilbara Minerals' Climate Change Position Statement, first issued in 2020, made a public statement of the Company's support for the recommendations of the TCFD.

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals continued to implement the TCFD recommendations by:

- Continuing to undertake scenario analysis to understand the strategic impacts of climate-related risks.
- Further embeded the management of climate change risks in the Company's Risk Register.
- Progressed the development of a net zero emissions pathway (scope 1 and 2).

## EMISSIONS REDUCTION: NET ZERO PATHWAY

Pilbara  Minerals  net  zero  pathway  was  developed  in  FY2021  based  on  outputs  from  a  series  of  workshops  with stakeholders. Pilbara Minerals' Board and Sustainability Committee have played a pivotal role in ensuring the net zero pathway options incorporate current and future growth. They have set an objective to embed emission reduction where practicable into every-day business and decision making to ensure the pathway can be achieved.

In FY2022, the net zero pathway was progressed and continues to be matured to identify the best fit pathway for the business. The following considerations are incorporated into the net zero pathway:

- Projected emissions profile of the Project (including expansion and efficiency projects).
- Alignment of emission reduction with a science-based emissions pathway.
- Staged approach for implementation of abatement projects to align with technology.
- Development of interim and final targets.

Initial  efforts  are  focused  on  emissions  reductions  from  plant  and  operation  efficiencies.  The  subsequent  emission reduction phases are more difficult to complete and will require a move from the current energy source (diesel) and adapting to a more efficient or renewable energy source. Pilbara Minerals is dedicated to meeting this challenge and will endeavour to implement the following initiatives to achieve its net zero goal.

The net zero pathway breakdown:

- Early opportunities.
- » Operational efficiencies - smaller scale renewable energy (6 MW solar plant), energy efficiencies within plant and haulage operations.
- » Mid-stream Project  -  construction  of  an  interim  lithia  salts  demonstration  facility  to  advance  the  technical development path of the technology.
- Mid-term opportunities.
- » Power generation - inclusion of alternative energy sources to offset diesel.
- » Materials  movement  -  materials  movement  fleet  (haul/excavate/load)  electrified  and  powered  alternative energy source to diesel.
- » External grid connection - interconnection to the North West Interconnected System ( NWIS ) which continues to advance a parallel pathway to decarbonisation.
- Long-term opportunities.
- » Mid-stream Project commercial scale-up - combined with low-carbon NWIS energy, this has the potential to significantly reduce the scope 3 profile within the lithium materials supply chain.
- » Further reduction via efficiencies and potentially carbon offsets - residual fugitive processes abated; potential to reduce hard to abate emissions through purchase of offsets.

Some of the actions, including construction of the 6 MW solar plant and operational efficiencies, are already underway, whereas others will be dependent on technological developments becoming available. Pilbara Minerals is committed to continuing to develop the processes, systems and operational changes that are required to adapt to the climate change challenge.

<!-- image -->

Pathway to Net Zero

## LIFE CYCLE ASSESSMENT

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals completed a life cycle assessment ( LCA ) to quantify the scope 1, 2 and 3 emissions within its value chain. The work supported Pilbara Minerals' approach to understand the source of its emissions and identify initiatives to further decarbonise its operations and areas within its value chain.

Pilbara Minerals worked with Minviro, global experts in battery metals LCA, to build ISO-compliant LCA models of the mining, processing, transport and conversion process to produce a battery grade LHM product. This work incorporated various other scenarios including renewable energy applications and differing conversion processes. The intent of the LCA was to identify and quantify emissions and environmental impacts from the point of resource extraction (at the Project) to produce a battery grade LHM product at a chemical conversion facility in China.

<!-- image -->

The output of the LCA is represented in global warming potential,  which  is  measured  in  kg  of  CO₂-e  per  kg  of battery grade lithium hydroxide monohydrate. This breakdown  into  scope  1,  2  and  3  emissions  provides Pilbara  Minerals  with  a  holistic  understanding  of  the carbon  emissions  profile  up  to  the  point  of  producing a  battery  grade  lithium  hydroxide  monohydrate.  This understanding  is  useful  as  Pilbara  Minerals  continues to  pursue  its  decarbonisation  pathway  and  works  on initiatives further down the supply chain.

The  total  global  warming  potential  for  Pilbara  Minerals' mined  spodumene  ore  through  its  value  chain  to  a battery grade lithium hydroxide chemical is similar to that of other hard-rock miners in Western Australia.

Global warming potential - scope 1, 2, 3

<!-- image -->

The LCA has identified opportunities for Pilbara Minerals to  investigate  to  reduce  emissions  within  its  operations and within sections of the lithium chemical supply chain, including innovative technologies within the POSCO JV, the Mid-stream Project and renewable energy sources.

## CARBON FOOTPRINT

The evaluation and monitoring of Pilbara Minerals' carbon  footprint  was  enhanced  in  FY2022  with  the implementation  of  a  continuous  emissions  monitoring system ( CEMS ). The CEMS allows for emissions data to be reviewed and tracked on a more regular basis. This enables the  Company  to  identify  efficiencies  and  trends  more readily  which  will  support  the  prioritisation  of  emission reduction initiatives. The CEMS is an important tool as the Company continues to pursue its aim of reaching net zero emissions in the decade commencing 2040.

## Scope 1 and 2 emissions

In  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals'  operations,  including  the Pilgan  and  Ngungaju  mine  and  plant  facilities  and  the Perth  offices,  produced  a  total  of  106,000  t  CO₂-e.  The increase in total emissions in FY2022 from the previous year (FY2021 total emissions were 60,066 t CO₂-e) can be attributed  to  the  larger  operational  footprint,  as  Pilbara Minerals  commissioned  and  began  operations  at  the Ngungaju Plant and increased overall activity in response to improved lithium market conditions.

The majority of the emissions can be attributed to the use of diesel, which is the main energy source used to power the  two  facilities,  associated  mine  fleet  and  supporting mine  and  camp  infrastructure.  The  remoteness  of  the Project  means  it  does  not  have  access  to  grid  power, therefore  stand-alone  power  generation  is  required  to support the operation.

Scope 1 and 2 GHG emissions are calculated in accordance with  the  Australian  Government  methodology  required by the National Greenhouse and Energy Reporting Act (2007) ( NGER  Act )  and  the  National  Greenhouse  and Energy Reporting scheme ( NGERS ).

The following figures and tables provide a breakdown of Pilbara Minerals' scope 1 and 2 GHG emissions quantities by operating facility.

## FY2022 GHG emissions by operating facility

## GHG emissions (t CO₂-e)

| Pilgan scope 1  84,000*  Ngungaju scope 1  22,000*  Total scope 1  106,000*  Total scope 2  58*   |
|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

*Based on internal data and calculations. The complete data will be provided in the NGER FY2022 report.

Pilbara Minerals understands that to meet its commitment to reach net zero in the decade beginning 2040, it  must  begin  the  path  to  reducing  its  emissions. As such, Pilbara Minerals' effort in FY2023 will be to finalise its emissions reduction pathway to ensure the objectives and actions are in place to support this journey.

## CASE STUDY SOLAR PLANT

Construction  of  the  6  MW  solar  array  power  plant  at  the  Project  is  nearing  completion,  with installation of the solar panels well underway. Pilbara Minerals is targeting commissioning of this facility in the fourth quarter of the 2022 calendar year.

Once  operational,  the  solar  power  plant  will  displace  approximately  3.8M  litres  ( l )  per  year  of diesel fuel, saving an estimated 9,900 t of CO₂-e annually during the contract period of 15 years. This  initiative  demonstrates  Pilbara  Minerals'  commitment  to  implementing  environmentally friendly power solutions, as a part of the Company's aim to transition to net zero emissions in the decade commencing 2040.

FINANCIAL REPORT

## ENVIRONMENT

AND BIODIVERSITY

## Enhancing, protecting and rehabilitating the environments where we operate

Pilbara Minerals values the diverse environments in which it operates and is committed to managing its impacts on these important ecosystems. For Pilbara Minerals, 'Environment and Biodiversity' includes the responsible consumption of water sources, resource management, as well  as  protecting  the  ecological values, where practicable.

Environmental compliance is consistently front of  mind,  as  is  improving  systems  and  processes  to manage the Project's expansion which will involve more people and higher production outputs. With  this  in  mind,  the  Environment  team  has been  working  to streamline  the  Environmental Management System ( EMS )  to  support  the  Project and the growing scope of the operation.

A focus for FY2022 has been on preparing environmental approvals for the Project's expansion, which includes an increasing footprint on the environment and additional use of resources, such as water and energy. The Company has ensured its EMS, policies and procedures consider the impacts of this expansion to ensure any adverse impacts on the environment are mitigated or minimised.

## ENVIRONMENT AND BIODIVERSITY OF THE PILBARA

The Pilbara spans 507,896 square kilometres (km2), much of which has a variety of endemic and introduced  flora  and  fauna  species  and  consists of  mangroves,  grasslands,  mountain  ranges  and gorges.  It  experiences  hot  summers,  mild  winters and low rainfall.

## ENVIRONMENT AND BIODIVERSITY MANAGEMENT SYSTEMS

Pilbara  Minerals  implements  an  'identify,  monitor, review and control' risk strategy to ensure any identified risks are monitored and controlled appropriately. With global policy developments such as the Taskforce on Nature-related  Financial  Disclosures  ( TNFD )  turning the  spotlight  onto  biodiversity  for  the  mining  and minerals sector, Pilbara Minerals wants to ensure that its EMS framework guides operations while delivering environmental  protection  and  responsible  resources consumption. Pilbara Minerals has an Environmental Policy, an EMS manual and an environmental management plan all of which provide direction and guidance towards fulfilling the Company's compliance obligations.

With the rapid and significant growth of the Company, Pilbara Minerals is working to further align with  the  international  standard  for  environmental management systems (ISO14001). In 2022, an external consultant  was  engaged  to  complete  an  audit  of the  EMS,  including  a  gap  assessment  against  the requirements of ISO14001, with the goal of being ready for certification in FY2023.

## LAND USE

Pilbara Minerals recognises that mining is a temporary use  of  land  and  therefore  planning  needs  to  be incorporated  into  management  processes  to  ensure understanding  of  potential  impacts  so  the  area  can be used for other purposes when mining has ceased. Clearing  and  land  disturbance  is  carefully  managed, and  Traditional  Owners  are  engaged  to  conduct heritage  assessment  and  monitor  sites  during  and after  activities.  Additionally,  the  local  pastoralist  is engaged to support land and animal management.

Pilbara Minerals utilises an internal land use certificate  process  to  ensure  any  disturbance  meets environmental,  cultural  and  approval  requirements. With  careful  land  management,  planning,  mining and knowledge sharing the Project can complement neighboring activities.

<!-- image -->

The  Pilbara  region's  high  biodiversity  and  landscape values are recognised internationally and protected through a range of conservation estate status. The nearest conservation  area  to  the  Project,  Mungaroona  Range Nature Reserve, is located 78 km to the southwest.

Recognising the environmental management measures put in place and the environmental values of the site, the Project was not subject to formal environmental impact assessment  under  the Environmental  Protection  Act 1986 (WA)  ( EP  Act )  or  the Environment  Protection  and Biodiversity Conservation  Act 2000 (Cth).  The Project is subject to ongoing environmental regulation under both the Mining  Act  1978 (WA)  and EP  Act ,  administered  by the Department of Mines, Industry and Regulation Safety ( DMIRS ) and the Department of Water and Environmental Regulation ( DWER ) respectively.

Pilbara  Minerals  is  committed  to  effectively  managing and  safeguarding  the  biodiverse  region  of  the  Pilbara through the Company's Environmental Policy, EMS and processes.  The  Company  is  also  committed  to  working with Traditional Owners to understand potential impacts and incorporate traditional knowledge  to ultimately enhance and rehabilitate  the  surrounding  environment through planning and active management.

## MINE CLOSURE

Closure and reclamation of a mining asset is complex and can be a challenging transition for any business, environment and stakeholders. Planning for closure commences  at  the  feasibility  stage  and  continues throughout  the  mine's  operational  life  to  identify and  reduce  risks  and  unknowns  over  time.  Pilbara Minerals  recognises  that  strong  engagement  and ongoing consideration  of  closure  from  the  Project's development  phase  will  help  to  mitigate  risks  and identify opportunities to leave a positive legacy.

The  Project  is  governed  by  the  Pilgangoora  Mine Closure Plan which is overseen by DMIRS. In FY2022, Pilbara  Minerals  lodged  the  triennial  review  of  its Pilgan Mine Closure Plan. This review included identification of the mine activities, associated risks, costs, and local community engagement.

## WATER USE

Pilbara Minerals understands that mining operations have the potential to impact upon water supply and quality and that these impacts need to be managed appropriately  especially  in  the  dry,  water-stressed environment of the Pilbara.

In  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  upgraded  its  pumping capacity at the Project to improve the recovery and reuse of water from the tailings dam decant back into the process water dam. Water recovery infrastructure including  drains  and  sediment  ponds  for  capture, recovery and supply of water to the Pilgan Plant were also  installed.  These  improve  water  use  efficiencies and assist to reduce water abstraction volumes from subsurface aquifers.

## TAILINGS MANAGEMENT

The responsible management of tailings facilities is a high priority  for  Pilbara  Minerals.  The  activity  of  mining  and processing  spodumene only  extracts  a  small  portion  of the volume of material introduced to the processing plant. The remaining spodumene-depleted ore is transferred as a slurry to two dedicated tailings management facilities ( TMFs ). These TMFs  provide permanent  storage for tailings generated from the process plants. The TMFs are engineered, designed and constructed to provide secure storage of fine tailings as a permanent landform.

The  operations  and  management  of  the  TMFs  occurs within  the  guidelines  and  boundaries  of  regulations and  codes  of  practice,  such  as  the  Australian  National Committee on Large Dams Guidelines on Tailings Dams (2012).

## CASE STUDY

## NYAMAL CAMP NAMES

In early 2022, Pilbara Minerals worked with Nyamal representatives to rename accommodation blocks at the Carlindi Camp. The new names align with the Nyamal words for local fauna. The blocks are now known as: Joodu (Snake); Marloo (Kangaroo); Warringpirna (Wedgetail Eagle); and Nyamela (Pink Witchetty Grub).

## WASTE MANAGEMENT

Mining  and  processing  plants,  and  associated  support activities,  produce waste materials that Pilbara Minerals carefully manages  through its Waste  Management Standard. In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals focused on increasing recycling and reducing the amount of landfill waste at its source. Some activities undertaken included:

- Removing reusable plastic food and drink containers from the Project.
- Donating  surplus  equipment  to  local  community and schools.
- Recycling of scrap metal, drill rods, waste oil, cans and glass.
- Continuing the rollout of the Containers for Change program.
- Routine landfill and site clean-ups by employees.

HEALTH AND SAFETY

## Building the culture, systems and processes to ensure everyone goes home safe and well every day.

'Safety  Matters'  is  central  to  Pilbara  Minerals'  operational  philosophy  and nothing is more important than ensuring every person goes home safe and well every day.

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals' strong commitment to reducing harm to employees was demonstrated through improvements in operational discipline, frontline leadership  expectations  and  training,  system  maturity,  as  well  as  improved investigation and incident management.

The FY2022 Total Recordable Injury Frequency Rate ( TRIFR )  result  increased to 4.4, from 3.6 in FY2021. A contributor to this result is the significant increase in personnel hours moving from FY2022 (2,048,370 hrs) compared to FY2021 (837,353 hrs). The Long-Term Injury Frequency Rate ( LTIFR ) remained at zero during the year, this can be attributed to the reduction in severity of injuries at the Project.

Total Recordable Injury Frequency Rate. Based on 1,000,000 hours

Pilbara Minerals

## SAFETY MANAGEMENT FRAMEWORK

Pilbara Minerals' Safety Management Framework provides the structure for implementing health and safety controls throughout the business. The Safety Management Framework and its supporting standards, guidelines and procedures  are  under  continual  review.  In  FY2022,  the entire management system was reviewed to reflect the integration of the Ngungaju operations.

In 2022, Western Australia implemented new workplace health  and  safety  ( WHS )  laws  and  regulations  to  align with  other  jurisdictions.  In  line  with  these  changes, Pilbara  Minerals  has  completed  an  independent  review of its Safety Management Framework against the requirements of the revised Health and Safety Management  System  ( HSMS )  as  described  within  the new  WHS  laws  and  created  a  forward  plan  to  ensure alignment with these changes.

During FY2022, Pilbara Minerals continued to independently  audit  its  workplace  health  and  safety performance against industry best practice. Results of the annual  auditing  are  utilised  for  continual  improvement of  health  and  safety  processes  and  practices  and  were reported to the Board.

## SITE HEALTH AND SAFETY COMMITTEE

The Project has a site-based Health and Safety Committee that incorporates members from operations and  the  supporting  functions.  These  health  and  safety representatives support implementation of the HSMS and help  to  promote  a  health  and  safety  culture  within  the site-based workforce. Central to their role is the review of safety incidents, supporting operational team members and feedback to site management.

## SAFETY TRAINING

In FY2022, Pilbara Minerals expanded  its site-based training capabilities and online training system to support the  growing  and  changing  workforce.  The  Company introduced new operations-focused training roles to ensure  that  appropriate  training  and  competencies  are maintained  in  accordance  with  the  operations  training needs analysis and regulatory requirements.

For personal use only

## SAFETY CULTURE

Pilbara Minerals is committed to providing a safe, inclusive workplace for all. Pilbara Minerals' safety culture is founded upon  empowering  and  supporting  its  employees  and recognises the core role they play in maintaining a safe work environment.

In FY2022, the Parliamentary Inquiry into sexual harassment  against  women  in  the  fly-in  fly-out  ( FIFO ) mining  industry  and  subsequent  'Enough  is  Enough' Report,  exposed  some  serious  breaches  of  codes  of conduct  within  the  Western  Australian  FIFO  mining industry.  In  response,  Pilbara  Minerals  worked  with  its peers  (through  the  Chamber  of  Minerals  and  Energy (CME))  on  an  industry  level  response  to  the  issue.  This work  involved  the  development  of  an  implementation plan on how to minimise harm, set proactive strategies and create a safer workplace for all.

For Pilbara Minerals, a strong safety culture includes being proactive  and  adaptable  and  responding  to  industry recommendations.

Pilbara Minerals endorses the 'Enough is Enough' Report  and  the  CME  response  and  has  implemented recommendations  in  line  with  the  findings,  including updating  its  responsible  service  of  alcohol  policy  to limit  alcohol  consumption  on  site,  mandatory  sexual harassment and workplace behaviour training, increasing security at its camps and promoting internal campaigns that support people to 'speak up' when they see or hear any unsafe actions or disrespectful behaviour.

<!-- image -->

## SAFETY RISK AND PERFORMANCE

As part of Pilbara Minerals' commitment to continual improvement, high-risk health and safety activities captured in the Company's Risk Register, including site risk registers, are routinely reviewed to ensure risks are ranked appropriately and controls are implemented and effective.

A key improvement in the risk performance during FY2022 came from the introduction of charter flights for site-based employees, operating between Perth and Wodgina Airport. This location is significantly closer to the Project, therefore dramatically reduces the risk of employees driving on heavily trafficked roads. Other key risk areas and actions in FY2022 are shown below.

Major risks and FY2022 actions

## ENHANCING THE QUALITY OF FIELD SAFETY INTERACTIONS

Field safety interactions are a key leading safety indicator. Pilbara  Minerals  has  set  an  internal  benchmark  of  two interactions per 1,000 hours of work completed to drive performance and focus. To meet this measure, focus has been  improvements  in  the  tools  that  measure  safety performance,  as  well  as  assisting  frontline  leaders  in understanding  and  managing  risk  during  work  tasks. These  new  tools  have  enabled  a  quality  measure  to  be established then reported to line leaders with real time data that focuses on key areas of risk as the risk emerges.

## CASE STUDY

## CONSTRUCTING AND COMMISSIONING NEW EMERGENCY RESPONSE FACILITIES

The increased activity at the Project, required an increase in the Company's emergency response capability. Pilbara Minerals  places  a  high  level  of  focus  and  funding  to support  this  capability  in  this  area  including  purchase of  additional  fixed  asset  and  mobile  assets  such  as  the medical  facility,  ambulances  and  fire  appliances.  An additional benefit of these improvements has been a 32% increase  in  Emergency Response volunteer numbers at the operations.
