## MENTAL HEALTH TRAINING SESSIONS

FIFO Focus psychologists delivered a suite of mental health training sessions at the Project at the end of the 2021 calendar year as part of Pilbara Minerals' commitment to creating a mentally healthy workplace and a resilient workforce.

The sessions aimed to increase mental health literacy and empower employees and contractors to look after themselves and each other. Separate sessions were conducted for leaders which addressed ways in which they can build an inclusive workplace that fosters positive mental health and wellbeing.

The mental health training sessions were a key initiative in Pilbara Minerals' Mental Health Strategy.

## TRADITIONAL OWNER RELATIONSHIPS AND OPPORTUNITIES

## Partnering and engaging with Indigenous people, particularly Traditional Owners, on economic and social opportunities and supporting connection to country.

Pilbara  Minerals  acknowledges  and  respects  Aboriginal people, their cultures, languages, traditions and connection  to  country,  and  actively  works  to  create mutual understanding and  strengthen  engagement throughout its business. The Company is committed to working  with  Traditional  Owners  to  ensure  meaningful engagement through employment and business enterprise  development  opportunities  arising  from  the Project.

## ENGAGEMENT WITH TRADITIONAL OWNERS

The Pilbara Minerals Stakeholder Engagement Framework  follows the International Association for Public Participation's principles for public participation. As such, Pilbara Minerals works closely with key stakeholders, including Traditional Owners of the land to identity what matters most to each key stakeholder group.

This close relationship with the Traditional Owners of the Pilgangoora region is demonstrated through the regular engagement  that  Pilbara  Minerals  has  with  individual Traditional Owners and their representative bodies. Examples include:

- Direct  engagement  with  the  Nyamal  Aboriginal Corporation Board ( NAC ).
- Site visitations by Nyamal and Kariyarra elders.
- Engagement of Traditional Owner business enterprises at Pilgangoora.
- Ongoing cultural awareness sessions for employees (Perth and site).

## DETERMINED LANDS AND NATIVE TITLE

Native Title in Australia is the common law system that recognises  the  pre-existing  rights  and  interests  to  land and waters held by Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples under traditional laws and customs. The Native Title  Act  1993 governs  the  determination  of  native  title claims in  Australia,  that  is  made  by  the  National  Native Title  Tribunal.  This  includes  claims  made  by  Aboriginal peoples in the Pilbara region.

The Project is located on the Native Title determined land of the Nyamal and Karriyarra Peoples with the majority of the operations located on Nyamal land. Pilbara Minerals entered into a Native Title Agreement with the Nyamal People in December 2016 and infrastructure agreements with the Kariyarra people. Both agreements are actively implemented through an Indigenous engagement strategy, as well as cultural heritage  management plans.  Compliance  with  these  agreements  is  reported to the Nyamal and Karriyarra Peoples on a regular basis throughout the year.

Pilbara Minerals' corporate office is located in the Perth metropolitan area, where Whadjuk-Noongar people are recognised as Traditional Owners. In 2021, the Noongar people  and  the  State  Government  negotiated a comprehensive native title agreement, the South West Settlement Agreement.

## CULTURAL HERITAGE MANAGEMENT

Pilbara Minerals recognises the important role it plays in honouring  and  safekeeping  both  Nyamal  and  Kariyarra heritage and is pro-active with its compliance under the Aboriginal Cultural Heritage Act 2021 (ACH Act). Pilbara Minerals,  in  consultation  with  Nyamal,  has  developed  a Cultural Heritage Management Plan ( CHMP ) that covers the Project.

Implementation  of  CHMP  is  overseen  by  the  Project's Indigenous  Liaison  Officer,  a  senior  traditional  owner, endorsed  by  the  Nyamal  people.  Activities  undertaken in accordance with the CHMP include site archaeological and  ethnographic  surveys,  pre-vegetation  monitoring surveys,  ongoing  cultural  heritage  site  inspections  and maintenance of heritage protection infrastructure. These activities are reported directly to NAC and are endorsed and supported.

Pilbara Minerals

## AGREEMENTS

Pilbara  Minerals  has  implemented  Heritage  Protection Agreements  for  regional  exploration  and  infrastructure activities  with  both  the  Nyamal  and  Kariyarra  Peoples. These agreements protect Traditional Owners access to traditional country and ensures accountability and sound management of heritage sites.

Pilbara  Minerals  also  works  closely  with  pastoral  lease holders  on  natural  resources  use,  such  as  water,  and land  management,  as  well  as  land  access.  Pastoral lease  holders  of  the  Project  area  have  long-standing relationships with the local Aboriginal communities and it  is  important that the Company, pastoral lease holders and  local  communities  all  contribute  to  conversations regarding land stewardship.

## ENGAGEMENT AND BENEFITS

Pilbara  Minerals  values  the  communities  in  which  it operates, and through the Project, the Company provides direct and indirect benefits to local communities.

As a first point of contact, Pilbara Minerals provides NAC with tender and contracting opportunities through the  Nyamal  Implementation  Strategy  for  Contracting and  Employment.  This  ensures  Nyamal  businesses  and individuals  receive  timely  invites  to  apply  for  suitable contracting and employment opportunities. Early offers  are  extended  to  businesses  that  have  a  high Nyamal or Karriyarra workforce. All suppliers are subject to  an  Indigenous  participation  review  as  part  of  the onboarding  process  and  as  stated  within  the  terms  of the  Company's  Native  Title  Agreement.  As  a  result,  in FY2022  approximately  $4.4M  was  awarded  to  various Nyamal  businesses  through  sole  contracts  and  under joint venture arrangements.

## RECONCILIATION

Pilbara Minerals routinely supports the movement towards reconciliation and the framework that is governed  by  the  national  peak  body,  Reconciliation Australia.  Pilbara  Minerals  is  considering  developing  a Reconciliation Action Plan ( RAP ).  The Company plans to engage with Reconciliation Australia on this issue during FY2023.

## KNOWLEDGE SHARING

Pilbara Minerals provides a comprehensive Cross-Cultural Awareness Training session, delivered by Nyamal Elders, to all employees and contractors, which is in addition to the  employee  induction  process.  Supplementing  this, non-Nyamal  Indigenous  employees  receive  a  Welcome to  Country  by  Nyamal  members,  which  is  a  culturally appropriate  way  to  introduce  Indigenous  people  from other areas to working on Nyamal Country.

In mid-December 2021, Pilbara Minerals' employees took part in Cross Cultural Awareness training in Perth and at the Project. The training is a pivotal way Pilbara Minerals supports  improving  employees'  understanding  of  and respect for Indigenous culture, language and traditions. The training was delivered over two days by senior Nyamal Elders and is continuing to be delivered throughout 2022.

During  the  year,  an  advanced  comprehensive  CrossCultural  Awareness  on  Country  session  was  planned. The  first  session  took  place  during  August  2022,  with Traditional Owner Elders hosting employees at an overnight  camp.  Participants  experienced  storytelling, traditional foods/cooking and participated in food gathering activities.

## CASE STUDY

## CULTURAL HERITAGE SURVEY

Heritage sites are of immense cultural, scientific, educational and historic interest and provide Traditional Owner partners, the Nyamal People, with an important link  to  their  present,  and  past  a  culture.  Working  with Nyamal  people  to  promote  their  culture  and  protect and manage places and objects of significance is a key priority for Pilbara Minerals and consistent with governing regulations.

## CASE STUDY

## NAIDOC WEEK

Across  Australia,  NAIDOC  week  celebrates  the  history, cultures and achievements of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples. To commemorate this significant week, Pilbara Minerals hosted a range of activities both on site and in the Perth Office.

At the Project, the Environment team arranged a number of  displays  at  Tambrah  and  Carlindi  camps,  including  a NAIDOC table talker poster, bush tucker fact sheets and an acknowledgment of Nyamal Country.

There  were  also  images  of  local  engravings  on  display at the front of both messes. Site Administration put the colourful artwork created by the Yandeyarra school kids on the walls in offices and in the dining areas.

Both  camp  service  providers, Civeo and  Asset link, included native Australian ingredients on their menus.

Nyamal and Pilbara Minerals have a Native Title Agreement covering the Pilgangoora Project area, which includes  heritage  protocols.  In  early  2022  a  team  of Traditional Owners with Nyamal and Strelley Community representatives  completed  heritage  clearance  surveys over areas that may be subjected to disturbance from the Company's exploration or mining operations.

By  completing  these  surveys,  Pilbara  Minerals  ensures any  areas  of  significance  remain  undisturbed  and  that appropriate safeguards are in place to preserve identified sites of significance for future generations.

The  Perth  office  was  visited  by  Kariyarra  elder,  Raylene Button,  who  shared  her  experiences  and  insights  in  an engaging  and  informative learning session. Raylene gave  a  personal  overview  of  historical  developments, cultural aspects and observations, gained from a growing resources  industry,  including  Pilbara  Minerals,  being  on Kariyarra country.

Pilbara Minerals' NAIDOC week activities provided helpful considerations  for  use  in  further  creating  a  workplace that understands,  values  and  respects  the  histories, cultures and contributions of Aboriginal and Torres Strait Islander peoples and further supports current and future Indigenous employees.

Other  NAIDOC  activities  included  the  attendance  of Pilbara  Minerals'  employees  at  NAIDOC  balls  in  Port Hedland and Perth.

EMPLOYEE ATTRACTION AND DEVELOPMENT

<!-- image -->

## Fostering a workplace culture that attracts and retains great people

Employee attraction and retention is an important factor for  Pilbara  Minerals  due  to  the  Project  expansion  and level  of  growth being experienced within the Company. As  Pilbara  Minerals  continues  to  scale  up  production, it  is  important  that  the  Company  provides  attractive opportunities for its current workforce as well as appeal to potential new talent.

Triggered  by  the  purchase  of  the  Ngungaju  Plant  and the  transition  to  owner-operated  mining  activities,  the Company  underwent  an  intense  recruitment  drive  to dramatically  increase  employee  headcount  throughout FY2022.

The initial  recruitment  drive  was  focused  on  employing four  crews  of  processing  personnel  for  the  Ngungaju Plant, whilst bolstering Operational Support Services and Maintenance  departments  as  well  as  some  Corporate roles, equating to an additional 85 roles.

The  recruitment  focus  shifted  to  mining  production  as the Company embarked on a shift towards owner miner. Phase 1 began in September 2021 with the first crew of Mobile Plant Operators and an additional 82 roles joining the workforce. Then, Phase 2 began in March 2022 with a further 81 roles coming on board.

## GRADUATE STRATEGY

Pilbara Minerals is working towards implementing its first formal  graduate  intake  and  development  program  to develop a pipeline of technical talent that will contribute to  the  growth of skills  within  the  industry.  This  will  also increase  the  Company's  exposure  to  potential  future talent while supporting its commitment to diversity and gender balance.

Pilbara  Minerals  will  be  seeking  to  fill  positions  within the  areas  of  processing,  mining  technical  and  asset management and reliability  over  the  coming  year  with the objective to launch a pilot program in early 2023, with a target to employ 50% female graduates.

## WORKPLACE CONDITIONS

Pilbara Minerals aims to provide market-leading employment  conditions  to  ensure  the  attraction  and retention of valuable people and skills and to promote an inclusively-friendly  site  and  village  environment  where employees feel respected, safe and supported.

During  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  continued  to  review employment  conditions  and  practices  to  help  ensure they are aligned with sector best practice, which included a fixed remuneration review in September 2021.

In April 2021, the Company introduced the Performance Incentive Scheme  ( the  Scheme ) for all  permanent employees  who  do  not  participate  in  managementlevel incentive schemes to drive performance outcomes and enhance attraction and retention of employees in a very competitive labour market. The Scheme also instills an  'owner's  mindset'  via  the  ability  for  employees  to elect to receive 50% of their achieved incentive payment in Performance Rights (Rights), with  the  Company contributing the same number of Rights that convert into shares following a 12-month deferred vesting period.

In recognition of the hard work, dedication and resilience shown by employees, the Company offered an end of year once-off payment of $1,000 as an Appreciation Bonus in December 2021. In January 2022, the Company introduced a monthly Lithium Bonus to directly and regularly share the success of the Company with its employees.

In  October  2021,  the  State  Government  introduced  a vaccination  mandate  for  mining  and  resources  sector workers,  which  came  into  effect  on  1  December  for first  vaccination  dose  and  to  be  fully  vaccinated  by  31 December  2021.  This  significantly  impacted  the  entire Western Australian labour market.

In  FY2022,  the  Company  saw  an  extremely  tight  labour market  with  fierce  competition  for  skilled  labour  and subsequently increased salaries.

To support employees through this period, particularly in consideration of the large proportion of new employees with small personal leave accruals, the Company implemented  a  COVID-19  Leave  and  Return  to  Work Policy  that  provided  paid  leave  (independent  of  paid personal leave) for employees who contracted COVID-19, or who had caring commitments to family members with COVID-19.

Previously  the  Company  introduced  a  Working  From Home (WFH) policy allowing Perth based employees to work from home one day per week. Approximately 40% of employees, across all levels, have taken this up. Feedback from  participants  is  that  this  initiative  has  allowed  an improved 'home/rest-of-life' balance.

## EMPLOYEE DEVELOPMENT AND CAREER PROGRESSION

As  part  of  the  Company's  commitment  to  continual improvement,  and  to  its  great  people,  Pilbara  Minerals strives to provide employees with opportunities to develop new skill sets and progress their career growth.

As a result, there were 33 internal promotions effected in FY2022.

2022 ANNUAL REPORT

Pilbara Minerals

## INCLUSION AND WELLBEING

Inclusion  and  wellbeing  are  central  to  Pilbara  Minerals' sustainability mission. Pilbara Minerals strives for positive impact through responsible and ethical actions, valuing its people and making sure everyone goes home safe and well every day. Delivering on this commitment includes actively supporting the wellbeing and safety of its people; fostering a safe and healthy workplace; and promoting an inclusive and diverse culture.

Pilbara Minerals is keenly aware that the mining industry in  Western Australia faces particular challenges relating to  the  under-representation  of  women,  the  prevalence of  sexual  harassment  and  pressure  on  mental  health. These challenges require long-term strategies and Pilbara Minerals will continue to work towards progress in these areas.

## EMBRACING A MENTALLY HEALTHY WORKPLACE

Continued  implementation  of  Pilbara  Minerals'  Mental Health  Strategy  has  been  a  target  for  FY2022.  The Company continued its engagement with FIFO Focus, a specialised psychological services firm based in Perth, to support its commitment towards developing a mentally healthy workplace. As the Company's mental health and wellbeing  partner,  FIFO  Focus  supported  the  Company during FY2022 in the following areas:

- Revision  of  the  Mental  Health  Strategy  to  ensure it aligns with Department of Mines, Industry Regulation and Safety ( DMIRS ) documents, Mentally Healthy Workplace for FIFO workers Code of Practice and Psychosocial Hazards Audit Technical Guide.
- Continuation  of  the  Mental  Health  First  Aiders program.
- Supervisor one-on-one coaching, leader education sessions, site people interactions.
- Established the Mental Health First Aider Working Group to energise mental health conversations.
- Implemented  improvements  to  the  onboarding process, to provide optimised communication between new starters and management.

## EMPLOYEE ENGAGEMENT

Ensuring Pilbara Minerals' people are well informed, and where possible involved in business decision making, is of the utmost importance to the Company's Leadership team. In September 2020, group sessions with all employees were held with the Executive Leadership team presenting  the  revised  Pilbara  Purpose  and  What  We Stand For statements.

For personal use only

As  a  result  of  these  engagement  sessions,  the  topic  of Diversity  and  Inclusion  was  highlighted  as  a  growing challenge for the sector. Pilbara Minerals engaged with the  workforce  to  reinforce  its  values  around  diversity and  inclusion,  especially  the  expectations  about  how everyone should be treated with respect. The deployment of the 'Stand Up - If You See Disrespect, Unmute Yourself' campaign  was  based  on  the  Company's  zero  tolerance approach towards disrespectful or inappropriate behaviour.  This  campaign  proved  to  be  a  segue  into supporting  the  broader  industry  response  to  stamping out sexual harassment

Steps involved with implementing the campaign involved:

- Written communications and on-site engagement sessions  between senior  leadership  members and employees.
- Discussions with senior leaders of contracting partners.
- 'Table  Talker'  signage  in  common  areas  to  help promote  and  reinforce  the  key  messages  and generate discussion amongst the team.
- Integration of the key messages  into ToolBox sessions and other communication tools.
- Contractor  engagement  at  all  organisation  levels (site and head office).

## SAFE AND RESPECTFUL BEHAVIOUR WORKSHOPS

Pilbara  Minerals'  employees  and  contractors  took  part in  a  series  of  Safe  and  Respectful  Behaviours  (SARB) workshops in FY2022 that focused on sexual harassment prevention in the workplace, with the intent of creating a safe and respectful culture where everyone feels included. A  total  of  60  workshops  were  held,  onsite  and  in  the Perth office, during April and May 2022, with almost 450 employees and key contractors attending the sessions.

The SARB awareness and leadership workshops helped  employees  and  contractors  to  understand  what constitutes acceptable and unacceptable behaviour, the Company's expectation of its workforce with a focus on 'speaking up', what steps to take if people experience or witness unacceptable behaviours, and an overview of the laws and duties related to sexual harassment.

Since  the  launch  of  the  'Unmute  Yourself'  campaign  in 2021, Pilbara Minerals has actively encouraged employees to  speak  up  and  take  action  if  they  see  or  experience sexual harassment in the workplace. The campaign has continued to involve the use of key messages in Toolbox sessions and other communication tools including 'Table Talker' signs in common areas to generate discussion.

## DIVERSITY AND INCLUSION

During  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  made  the  following progress with regards to diversity and inclusion:

- Continued focus on boosting female employment through 'shortlisting commitments' in the Recruitment and Selection Procedure. The percentage  of  female  representation  across  the total employee workforce increased by 2% from 22% to  24%.  -  This  increase  occurred  through  a  period growth where the employee count grew 266% (from 170 - 452).
- Increased uptake of Parental Leave for employees in  the  first  twelve  months  following  childbirth, there was an uptake by 11 employees - 5 male and 6 female.
- Working from home arrangements for Perth office  employees  was  continued,  with  excellent feedback  from  employees  who  reported  that  the arrangements better supported their work/life balance.
- Continued  implementation  of  the  Diversity  and Inclusion Policy and measurement of its impact.
- Continued to provide early notifications to Nyamal Traditional Owners  to  ensure awareness  of all opportunities.

In  addition,  the  Company  introduced  a  gender  balance target  in  its  FY2022  Short-Term  Incentive  (STI)  scheme for executive, senior and middle managers comprising of between 20% (on-target achievement) and 25% (stretch achievement)  female  employment  rates  for  all  new operations  positions;  for  which  the  Company  achieved 22% female employment.

Pilbara  Minerals  implemented  a  range  of  measures  in FY2022 to foster an inclusive working environment that aligns with the Company's culture including identifying additional  PPE  options  for  female  employees,  provision of  gender-inclusive  activities  at  sites,  changes  to  the Responsible Service of Alcohol at sites in line with industry guidance  as  well  as  the  addition  of  questions  relating to  safe  and  respectful  behaviours  into  the  recruitment process.

## Working with our communities and people to create shared value

## COMMUNITY INVESTMENT STRATEGY

During FY2022, Pilbara Minerals developed a Community Investment Strategy ( CIS ) to guide investment in community  programs  and  events,  ensuring  they  align with  the  Pilbara  Purpose,  identified  impacts  of  the Project, and Pilbara Minerals' aspirations for responsible corporate citizenship.

The CIS, developed in recognition of the Pilbra Minerals' recent growth and in support of continual improvement of its corporate governance processes, covers:

- Communities of interest.
- Areas of focus.
- Program structure.
- Strategy development.
- Tools.

## Four key areas of focus:

- Education.
- Mental health and wellbeing.
- Community resilience.
- Energy transition.

Examples of community investment in these focus areas follows.

## Education

## PARTNERSHIP WITH YANDEYARRA SCHOOL

Pilbara  Minerals'  partnership  with  Yandeyarra  Remote Community School is part of the Community Investment Strategy to support education with the aim to improve opportunities and outcomes for local communities.

Pilbara  Minerals  entered  into  an  art  partnership  with Yandeyarra Regional Community School where students designed  artwork  to  be  placed  onto  the  cyclone  buffer screens at the Project.

In addition, Pilbara Minerals provided ongoing sponsorship of the 'Books in Homes' program to students.

SUSTAINABILITY

Pilbara Minerals

<!-- image -->

## STUDENTS MEET INDUSTRY CAREER EVENT

A  team  from  Pilbara  Minerals  headed  to  Kalgoorlie  to speak  with  students  at  The  Australasian  Institute  of Mining  and  Metallurgy's  Students  Meet  Industry  Night, where Pilbara Minerals was an event sponsor.

Pilbara  Minerals'  team  spoke  with  mining  engineering students  about  their  work  experiences  in  the  lithium mining sector and enabling the global energy transformation.

The  night  was  a  hit  with  the  students  and  industry participants alike and  provided  valuable  networking opportunities between students and industry partners.

## INTERNSHIP PROGRAM

Pilbara  Minerals  implemented  a  six-month  internship program for undergraduate university science students. This allows individual students to work part-time in the corporate office, while learning about different works and specialisations  available  for  environmental  professionals in the mining sector and have a first understanding of the environmental  and  mining  regulatory  frameworks.  The program included a site visit to the Project, to have a firsthand experience of what it means to be a FIFO worker, and the type of field work that the Environmental team do on site. Two students participated in FY2022.

2022 ANNUAL REPORT For personal use only

## Mental health and wellbeing

## LOCAL NOT-FOR-PROFIT ORGANISATIONS SUPPORT

In November, Pilbara Minerals sponsored suicide prevention  training  for  the  Youth  Involvement  Centre employees in South Hedland.

Shortly after the reporting period closed, Pilbara Minerals provided  funding  to  several  local  not-for-profit  groups including the Hedland Well Women's Centre, the Bloodwood Tree Association and the Royal Flying Doctor Service WA.

## COVID-19 REGIONAL COMMUNITY SUPPORT

In early March 2021, Pilbara Minerals donated 200 Rapid Antigen Test kits to the Yandeyarra Remote Community School and the Strelley Community School.

The RAT kits provided by Pilbara Minerals supported the communities to identify and reduce the risk of COVID-19 spreading  and  was  warmly  welcomed  by  the  local representatives.

Pilbara Minerals also helped promote the WACS regional vaccination program and donated a bed for the use at the clinics.

Later in the year, Pilbara Minerals contributed to providing non-perishable supplies to the local communities as they were  unable  to  travel  to  shops  in  Port  Hedland  due  to COVID-19 isolation protocols.

## LOCAL SPORTS

Pilbara Minerals sponsored several local sporting organisations during the year including the South Hedland Football Club and the Eagles Netball Club which helps maintain physical and mental fitness and builds a stronger sense of belonging and community.

2021 MACA Cancer Ride team

<!-- image -->

## Community resilience

## FACILITIES NAMING BY TRADITIONAL OWNERS

The Ngungaju and Pilgan plants are located on Nyamal country and were named by the Nyamal people.

The  camp that  supports  the  Ngungaju  Plant  is  located on  the  country  of  the  Kariyarra  people,  the  camp  was renamed  following  consultation with our Traditional Owners  and  is  now  known  as  Tambrah,  which  is  the traditional name of a nearby creek.

A naming ceremony for the Tambrah Camp was held on site  in  January.  The  Tambrah  Camp  houses  employees, contractors and visitors working at both the Pilgan and Ngungaju plants.

## EMPLOYEE VOLUNTEER AND FUND RAISING

Employees donned their lycra and walking shoes to raise funds for cancer research in the 2021 MACA Cancer Ride and 2022 Women's Walk for Cancer. Both teams raised over $30,000 for the Harry Perkins Institute and the cancer research they undertake.

Employees  also  rode  in  the  Hawaiian  Ride  For  Youth raising  much  needed  funds  to  address  youth  mental health, suicide and depression.

## STRELLEY COMMUNITY LITHIUM BATTERY PROJECT

Strelley  School  is  located  between  Port  Hedland  and the  Project  and  is  one  of  Australia's  oldest  continually operational Independent Aboriginal Community Schools. In  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  worked  on  a  partnership  to provide funding towards the installation of a combined 33 kW  PV and lithium battery storage system in partnership with Pacific Energy and the Strelley School and Community.

The construction of the solar panels and battery will occur in the 2023 calendar year. During installation community members  will  have  the  opportunity to learn  about renewable energy and maintenance of the facility which will be a community asset.

This renewable energy source will displace approximately 60,000  litres  of  diesel  usage  annually,  with  the  savings of approximately $20,000 per year to be re-purposed for education needs.

## BUSINESS ETHICS AND TRANSPARENCY

Operating honestly and ethically and complying with our licence to operate.

## CORPORATE GOVERNANCE

Pilbara  Minerals  understands  the  long-term  benefits of  maintaining  the  highest  standard  of  business  ethics and  good  governance  practices.  Pilbara  Minerals'  Code of Conduct supports the Company's values and provides a  framework  for  how  to  do  business.  The  Company  is committed to working in accordance and in compliance with  relevant  laws  and  regulations  in  all  jurisdictions  of operation.

Pilbara  Minerals  is  fully  committed  to  maintaining  and enhancing corporate governance which the Board and  management  agree  is  essential  for  the  long-term sustainability of the business. Responsibility for the Company's  strategy  and  governance  framework  rests with the Board and Management team, and is supported by three committees that assist the Board to effectively discharge its duties:

- Audit and Risk Committee.
- People and Culture Committee.
- Sustainability Committee.

The Company's governance framework also comprises various policies including the Code of Conduct; Continuous Disclosure; Securities Trading; Shareholder Communications and Investor Communications;  Compliance  Policy  and  Framework; Anti-Bribery  and  Corruption  Policy;  Confidentiality  and Conflict of Interest Protocols; Inclusion and Diversity Policy; Renumeration Policy and Framework; Risk Management Policy and Framework; and Whistleblower policies.

All policies and practices are reviewed on a regular basis and updated to ensure they support the current business environment  and  reflect  any  changes  to  relevant  ASX listing  rules  and  other  regulations.  Key  policies  and  charters reviewed during FY2022 included all Committee Charters, Risk  Management  and  Risk  Appetite  Frameworks,  and the Minimum Holding Condition Policy.

A full  version of current Board and Committee Charters and Corporate Governance Policies as well as the Corporate  Governance  Statement  for  FY2022  can  be viewed  in  the  Corporate  Governance  section  of  the Company's website at: pilbaraminerals.com.au.

## BOARD AND COMMITTEES

While the Board is responsible for overall governance of the Company,  it is assisted by the three  aforementioned  committees.  These  committees are  comprised  of  independent  non-executive  directors. The role of the committees is to review, advise and provide recommendations to the Board, where appropriate.

Each committee has a Charter which establishes its roles and  responsibilities,  and  these  Charters  are  regularly reviewed by the Committee and the Board. Each committee meets on a routine basis with a focus on the key responsibilities as shown in the following table.

## Committees and responsibilities

| Audit and Risk Committee  People and Culture Committee  Sustainability Committee  Corporate reporting processes  Remuneration  framework  and  performance  of  directors  and  senior  executives  Sustainability  Policy  and  its  progress  against  stated  sustainability  commitments  Financial  systems,  statements  and performance  External remuneration reporting  External sustainability reporting  Taxation  issues  including  tax  governance  Company culture  Sustainability risks and opportunities  Risk  Management  and  Compliance  Frameworks  and  systems  Diversity  and  inclusion  policies  and  procedures  Performance  of  environment  and  community policies and management  systems  Code of Conduct, Whistleblower  Policy  and  Anti-Bribery  and  Corruption Policy  Nomination  and  appointment  of  directors including appropriate balance  of  skills,  knowledge,  experience,  independence, and diversity  Native Title Agreement and land access  and heritage management plans  Audit functions  Performance  evaluation  process  of  the Board, individual directors and the  Managing Director  Climate change strategy  Insurance program  Induction  programs  and  continuing  professional development  Ethical business practices   |

## RISK MANAGEMENT

Pilbara Minerals approach to risk management is based on a well-established framework that routinely embeds risk management across all areas of the business.

The Board and Audit and Risk Committee not only contain a mix of skills that add value to the risk management function but also interface with management ensuring the best outcome for the Company. Board members regularly communicate with management in relation to risk and are actively involved in understanding emerging issues.

The Company recently adopted a three-line defence risk model.

| First line defence  Second line defence  Third line defence  Risk  Owners  who  own  and  manage the risk  Risk Department which oversees that  risk is being actively managed and acts  as the Subject Matter Expert (  SME  )  in  risk management practices  Internal  Audit  (via  appointed  audit  Consultant)  providing  an  independent  assurance that risks are managed   |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

The annual review of Pilbara Minerals' Risk Framework in FY2022 involved multiple workshops discussing pertinent risk  matters,  updates  to  the  Company's  Risk  Register,  briefing  sessions  and  endorsement  from  the  Audit  and  Risk Committee. In FY2022, the following improvements were implemented into the Company's system:

- Introduction of High Risk - Low Likelihood (HILL) indicators across the 5x5 risk matrix and governance to support risks in this category.
- Improvements on assessment of financial impacts and assumption basis.

•

- Additional commentary addressing risk control effectiveness.

New people trained in Company risk practices

New risks identified throughout the year and matured into the Company's Risk Register

## GRIEVANCE MECHANISMS

Pilbara  Minerals  trusts  its  employees  to  do  the  right thing and part of this is reporting any behaviour in the workplace  that they feel is wrong  or  inappropriate and  constitutes  Reportable  Conduct  as  defined  in  the Company's Whistleblower Policy.

Pilbara  Minerals  introduced  the  Whispli  tool  to  support internal and  external stakeholders to submit  issues that they believe involve criminal, inappropriate, or unprofessional  behaviour  in  FY2021.  In  FY2022,  this  tool was bolstered by ongoing communications from leadership and internal training.

## ECONOMICAL AND FINANCIAL

As  outlined  previously,  market  demand  conditions  for battery  raw  materials  improved  significantly  over  the course of FY2022. As a result, the Company was able to deliver record production, shipping and pricing outcomes. In  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  generated  revenue  of  $1.2B and a net profit after tax of $561.8M.

Pilbara Minerals continued to deliver a quality spodumene product to market and is pursuing a growth and diversification strategy to become a sustainable, lowcost lithium producer and a fully integrated lithium raw materials and chemicals supplier in the years to come.

Pilbara Minerals successfully:

- Commissioned Ngungaju Plant and installed improvements  at  Pilgan Plant to increase ore processing efficiency and capacity (which was supported by a US$20M increase to the Syndicated Finance Facility with existing lenders to US$130M).

•

- Progressed studies for the Mid-stream Project which will  support  the  development  of  a  demonstration plant  in  joint  venture  with  Calix  to  produce  value added less  carbon  intensive  lithium  phosphate  or other preferred salt products for global distribution.
- Formed a downstream JV with POSCO to construct and operate a 43,000 tpa lithium hydroxide chemical processing  facility  in  South  Korea.  The  Company owns  an  18%  equity  interest  in  the  JV  with  the option to increase its equity to 30%. The Company's 18% interest was fully funded by a $79.6M five-year convertible bond provided by POSCO.

The  Company  concluded  FY2022  with  a  strong  cash balance  of  $874.2M  (inclusive  of  $282.4M  of  irrevocable letters  of  credit  ( LoC )  for  shipments  completed  prior  to the  end  of  the  reporting  period),  a  significant  increase from FY2021.

## Key financial outcomes for FY2022

| Net profit after tax  $561.8M  Cash balance  $874.2M (30 June 2022 cash  balance of $591.7M plus  $282.4M LoC)  Receipts from  customers  $968.1M (spodumene  concentrate)  Royalties paid  $28.0M (government and  native title)  Salaries paid  $51.8M  Payment for goods  and services  $366.5M (operating and  capital, excluding salaries  royalties and taxes and net of  tantalite receipts)   |

## CASE STUDY

## CYBER SECURITY TRAINING

Cyber security is steadily becoming an increasing threat to  both  organisations  and  individuals.  Phishing  scams, Cyberespionage, theft of company or personal data and identity fraud are just some of the ways hackers can steal information, and they are getting better at it all the time. In 2020/2021 the Australian Cyber Security Centre received over 67,500 reports, equating to about one report every eight  minutes.  That's  a  lot  of  breaches  and  why  Pilbara Minerals  provided  many  of  its  employees  with  cyber security  training  in  FY2022,  along  with  updating  of  the Information Security Policy and Internet Usage Policy and guidance as to expectations of employees when it comes to cyber security.

## PROTECTING HUMAN RIGHTS

## Supporting and influencing positive change throughout our supply and value chains.

## SUPPLY CHAIN

In  FY2022,  Pilbara  Minerals  spent  approximately  $475M globally  on  goods  and  services  from  over  414  active suppliers. The Company's focus was on engaging with its Tier 1 suppliers, those with the largest spend, to support the ongoing understanding of their exposure to modern slavery and human rights.

## MODERN SLAVERY STATEMENT

Many  people  across  the  globe,  including  Australia,  are subjected to varying degrees of modern slavery.

Pilbara  Minerals  is  committed  to  making  a  meaningful contribution  to  mitigating  modern  slavery  throughout its  activities.  It  is  an  essential  aspect  of  Pilbara  Minerals' approach to  socially  responsible  business  practices  and integral  to  its  commitments  under  'What  We  Stand For'.  Modern  slavery  includes  slavery,  servitude,  human trafficking, debt bondage, child labour, deceptive recruiting for labour or services and forced or compulsory labour.

For Pilbara Minerals, procuring goods and services ethically is not only a socially responsible business practice,  but  also  a  regulatory  requirement  under  the Modern Slavery Act 2018 (Cth). This Act requires Australian companies with  annual  consolidated  revenue  of  $100M or  more  to  submit  a  modern  slavery  statement  to  the Australian Government on an annual basis.

Pilbara Minerals'  second  mandatory  Modern  Slavery Statement  is  being  finalised  in  preparation  for  lodging with  the  Australian  Government.  The  FY2022  Modern Slavery Statement shows that Pilbara Minerals continued to  mature  and  assess  its  modern  slavery  exposure  risks through  ongoing  review  of  its  internal  systems  and processes  and  greater  collaboration  with  suppliers  and customers.

<!-- image -->

Supply Chain Spend

During the year, the Company strengthened its Recommendation  for  Award  process,  which  includes supplier  initial  pre-qualification  risk  assessments  and ongoing  risk  assessments  for  suppliers  identified  as higher risk (determined by country of origin or industry sector).  Pilbara  Minerals  also  conducted  routine  reviews of the Company's Risk Register to ensure risks associated with modern slavery were supported by rigorous controls and managed effectively.

Pilbara  Minerals  is  continuing  to  take  proactive  steps in  reducing the risk of modern slavery within its supply chain  and  ensures  it  maintains  a  level  of  transparency throughout its business dealings. Even though its exposure is very low, the Company is committed to directly and indirectly protecting human rights and welfare. The Company's Modern Slavery Statements are available on its  website as well as the website of the Department of Home Affairs.

No instances of modern slavery were identified within the supply chain and no reported instances of modern slavery  were  identified  within  grievance  processes  in FY2022.

## IMPORTANT INFORMATION

Information in this report regarding expansions in nameplate  capacity of the Pilgan Plant and/or the Ngungaju  Plant  are  underpinned  by  the  Company's existing  Ore  Reserves  that  have  been  prepared  by  a Competent  Person  in  accordance  with  the  JORC  Code (2012  Edition)  and  were  released  by  the  Company  to ASX on 6 October 2021. The relevant proportions of Ore Reserves are 11% Proven Ore Reserves and 89% Probable Ore  Reserves.  The  Company  confirms  it  is  not  aware of  any  new  information  or  data  that  materially  affects the  information  included  in  that  release  or  report  and that  all  material  assumptions  and  technical  parameters underpinning  the  Ore  Reserves  estimates  continue  to apply and have not materially changed.

The scoping study referred to in this report in respect of the mid-steam project has been undertaken to determine the potential viability of the demonstration plant and to reach a decision to proceed with more definitive studies and  enter  into  a  JV  agreement  with  Calix.  The  scoping study  has  been  prepared  to  an  accuracy  level  of  +/40% (for Capital costs) and +/-30% (for Operating costs). The  scoping  study  is  based  on  low-level  technical  and economic  assessments  and  is  insufficient  to  provide assurance of an economic development case at this stage or provide certainty that the conclusions of the studies will be realised. The results of the scoping study should not be considered a profit forecast or production forecast 18 .

Although  the  Company  believes  that  its expectations, estimates and forecast outcomes are based on reasonable assumptions, it can give no assurance that they will be achieved. They may be affected by a variety of variables and changes in underlying assumptions that are  subject  to  risk  factors  associated  with  the  nature  of the  business,  which  could  cause  actual  results  to  differ materially from those expressed herein. ## DEFINITIONS

## Mineral Resources

Is  a  concentration  or  occurrence  of  material  of  intrinsic economic interest in or on the Earth's crust in such a form, quantity or quality that there are no reasonable prospects for eventual  economic  extraction.  The  location,  quality,  grade, geological characteristics and continuity of a Mineral Resource are known, estimated or interpreted from specific geological evidence and knowledge.

## Ore Reserves

Is  the  economically  mineable  part  of  a  Measured  and/ or  Indicated  Resource.  It  includes  diluting  materials  and allowances  for  losses,  which  may  occur  when  the  material is  mined.  Appropriate  assessments  and  studies  have  been carried  out  that  demonstrate  at  the  time  of  reporting  that extraction could be reasonably justified.

## PRINCIPAL ACTIVITIES

The  principal  activities  of  the  Group  during  the  year  were  the  exploration,  development  and operation  of  the  Pilgangoora  Project  and  Operations  (' Pilgangoora  Project ').  The  Pilgangoora Project includes two processing plants, being the Pilgan Plant and the Ngungaju Plant.

## Objectives

The Group's objectives are:

- to  produce  and  sell  to  customers  internationally,  spodumene  and  tantalite  concentrate products from the conduct of safe and sustainable mining and processing activities at the 100% owned Pilgangoora Project located in the Pilbara region of Western Australia;
- to continue expanding the production capacity of the Pilgangoora Project to match market demand;
- to  increase  the  existing  JORC  resource  and  reserve  at  the  Pilgangoora  Project,  through undertaking further exploration and development activities; and
- to  leverage  the  size  and  quality  of  the  Pilgangoora  Project  by  creating  strategic  growth opportunities  through  the  establishment  of  deeper  links  within  the  global  lithium  raw materials supply chain, including participation in downstream chemical processing opportunities and identifying innovations in value added mid-stream products.
- to further integrate our sustainability objectives into all facets of the Company.

In order to meet these objectives, the following targets have been set for the 2023 financial year and beyond:

8

- Commence construction of the P680 Project expansion to increase the annual nameplate production capacity of the Pilgan Plant from ~360-380,000 dry metric tonnes ('dmt') to ~460480,000  dmt  of  spodumene  concentrate  through  the  delivery  of  a  new  primary  rejection heavy  media  separation  circuit.  Once  completed,  the  combined  production  nameplate capacity  of  the  Pilgangoora  Project  (Pilgan  and  Ngungaju  plants)  is  expected  to  be  ~640680,000dmt.
- Commence  construction  to  replace  the  existing  contracted  crushing  facility  with  a  new integrated crushing and ore sorting facility to increase the ore throughput capacity of the Pilgan Plant to ~ 5.0Mtpa in support of future project expansions.
- Complete the ramp up and commissioning of the Ngungaju Plant to an annual full production capacity of 180-200,000 dmt of spodumene concentrate.
- Subject to market conditions and the ongoing demand for lithium raw materials, continue to undertake studies to incrementally expand the Pilgan Plant from 2.3Mtpa of ore up to ~ 5Mtpa, to ultimately produce ~800,000 dmt per annum of spodumene concentrate. Once completed, the  combined  Pilgangoora  Project  (inclusive  of  the  Ngungaju  Plant)  would  be  capable  of producing up to ~1Mtpa of spodumene concentrate.
- Safety and Environment:
- -continued improvement in the Company's safety performance as measured by leading and lagging indicators.
- -minimising the impact of our operations on the environment and surrounding communities.
- Sustainability:
- -Complete  sustainability  material  topic  and  strategy  refresh,  including  identification  of initiatives  and  target  setting  against  an  external  framework  to  improve  sustainability practices and performance.
- -continue to assess high-level decarbonisation pathway and undertake studies on initiatives to  effectively  and  efficiently  achieve  net  zero  emissions  (Scope  1  and  2)  in  the  decade commencing 2040, including implementation of internal mechanisms.
- -further evaluation of risks and subsequent planning to manage exposure in consideration of evolving policy, standards and regulations.
- -continue  to  proactively  engage  with  Traditional  Owners  and  provide  support  to  local communities.

## Diversification and value-added products:

- -jointly develop the 43ktpa LHM primary lithium hydroxide chemical processing facility in South Korea in joint venture with POSCO Holdings ( POSCO ),
- -progress the ongoing work with Calix to develop a demonstration plant to produce a 'valueadded' lithium phosphate or other preferred salts ( Mid-Stream Project )  that will further enhance the sustainability of the battery raw material supply chain.

## REVIEW OF OPERATIONS

## Key Highlights

- Increased revenue and operating margin attributable to significant increases in production and lithium raw material prices during the year, following surging market demand amid concerns about supply shortages for spodumene concentrate.
- Operating profit after tax for the year of $561.8M underpinned  by significant increase in the selling price and improved sales volumes of spodumene concentrate. The Company recognised tax benefits of $108.8M as a result of being able to recognise prior year tax losses.

9

- Favourable market demand conditions during the year supported higher revenue of $1.2B (FY21: $175.8M), with an average realised selling price of US$2,382/dmt CIF China.
- Cash balance at 30 June 2022 of $591.7M (FY21: $99.7M) achieved on the back of strong operating cashflows during the year.  Cash balance of $874.2M inclusive of $282.4M of irrevocable bank letters of credit for shipments completed up to 30 June 2022.
- 361,035  dmt  of  spodumene  concentrate  was  shipped  during  the  period,  a  28%  increase compared to the 281,440 dmt shipped for the year ending 30 June 2021.
- Improved  pricing  for  spodumene  concentrate  following  price  reviews  with  existing  offtake customers to better align product pricing with market conditions.
- Introduction  of  the  Battery  Material  Exchange  (BMX)  digital  sales  auction  platform,  with  six cargo auctions completed during the financial year. Exceptional pricing outcomes were realised, with the auction in June 2022 achieving a spot sale price of US$6,350/dmt (CIF China) on an equivalent SC6.0 basis.
- A 54% increase in the total Pilgangoora Proved and Probable Ore Reserves (JORC) to 162mt grading 1.2%Li2O, 100ppm Ta2O5 and 1.0% Fe2O3 (refer ASX release dated 6 October 2021).
- Successful commissioning of both the coarse and fines circuit of the Ngungaju Plant, with the plant in ramp up and expected to achieve name plate production capacity of 180-200ktpa of spodumene concentrate during the September Quarter 2022.
- Completion of the construction and commissioning of the Pilgan Plant Improvements Project during the year resulting in an increase to installed production capacity from 330ktpa to 360380ktpa of spodumene concentrate.
- Formation of a downstream joint venture (JV) with POSCO to construct and operate a 43ktpa LHM primary lithium hydroxide chemical processing facility in South Korea. Pilbara Minerals initial 18% equity participation of $76.2M in the JV was funded from a $79.5M 5-year convertible bond provided by POSCO.  Pilbara has an option to increase its equity participation to 30%.  As part of the deal, the Company entered into a 315ktpa offtake agreement with the JV.
- Progression of the Company's Mid-Stream Project as a key sustainability initiative, including completion of a scoping study and a binding Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) with Calix to establish a joint venture for the development of a demonstration plant to produce valueadded lower carbon lithium phosphate or other preferred salts products for global distribution, and if viable for the future commercialisation of the process;
- A  final  investment  decision  (FID)  made  to  expand  the  Pilgan  Plant's  nameplate  production capacity by a further 100ktpa (P680 Project) at an estimated cost of $103M;
- Decision to introduce ore sorting technology at the Pilgan facility, as well as replace the existing crushing  facility  with  a  larger  5Mtpa  company-owned  crushing  facility  to  support  future expansions, at a total estimated capital cost of $194M;
- Agreements with existing senior lenders to expand both the existing Syndicated Finance and Working Capital facilities to support the re-start of the Ngungaju Plant earlier in the year;
- Completion of a Life Cycle Assessment to lithium hydroxide production and development of a decarbonisation pathway to reach net zero in the decade commencing 2040; and
- A major contract award for the construction of a 6MW solar power plant at the Pilgangoora Project with completion expected by the December Quarter 2022, which is expected to reduce CO2 emissions by 9,000 tonnes.

10

## Pilgangoora Operation

Market demand conditions for battery raw materials improved significantly over the course of the year. As a result, the Company was able to deliver record production, shipping and pricing outcomes for  FY2022  despite  a  backdrop  of  challenging  business  conditions,  including  a  competitive  and constricted labour market in the West Australian mining sector, the impacts of COVID-19 illness on labour availability, inflationary cost pressures, and supply chain disruption.

During  the  year,  2.42  million  tonnes  of  ore  was  processed,  a  53%  increase  on  the  prior  year's production (previous year: 1.58 million tonnes) following the successful completion of the Pilgan Plant  Improvement  Project  and  restart  of  the  Ngungaju  Plant  (brought  out  of  care  and maintenance). 11

## Pilgan Plant

Spodumene concentrate production at the Pilgan Plant increased to 321,221 dmt (previous year 281,098 kmt).  Production volumes at the Pilgan Plant were impacted by resourcing shortfalls for employees  and  contractors  due  to  the  COVID-19  pandemic  and  the  tight  labour  market  in  the Western Australian mining industry.  Lithia recoveries were lower than planned impacted by lessthan-optimal ore feed to the plant following mining constraints, as well as the work required to tie in  the  plant  improvement  works.    Following  the  successful  commissioning  of  the  Pilgan  Plant Improvement Project in the December 2021 Quarter, installed production capacity at the Pilgan Plant increased on an annualised basis from ~330,000tpa to ~360,000-380,000 tpa of spodumene concentrate.  The Pilgan Plant performed to expectations following completion and commissioning of the improvement works.

A full year unit operating cost 2  (FOB Port Hedland and excluding freight and royalties) of A$555/dmt was achieved for the Pilgan operation (previous year: A$431/dmt).  Unit operating costs were affected by manning shortfalls translating to reduced mined volumes resulting in less-than-optimal ore feed to the plant impacting production and recoveries, as well as unplanned/extended shutdowns to the plant.  Inflationary pressures during the second half of the reporting period including increases in diesel fuel prices, ocean freight, reagent and labour costs (the latter stemming from the buoyant labour  market  for  the  WA  mining  industry  inclusive  of  bonus  structures  to  attract  and  retain employees).

Including freight and royalty costs, the unit operating cost for the year was A$844/dmt (previous year: A$519/dmt) with the significantly higher selling prices translating to substantially higher royalty costs (royalty cost FY22: A$194/dmt; previous year: A$37/dmt).

2 Unit operating costs (FOB Port Hedland excluding freight and royalties) include mining, processing, transport, native title costs, port charges and site-based general and administration costs and are net of Ta2O5 by-product credits. It is calculated on an incurred basis (inclusive of accruals) and include inventory movements and credits associated with capitalised deferred mine waste development costs.

## Ngungaju Plant

During the year the staged restart of the Ngungaju Plant commenced following Board approval in June 2021.  The restart was undertaken in two phases, starting with the coarse circuit followed by the fines circuit.  First spodumene concentrate from the coarse production circuit was achieved in October 2021, with the fines production circuit commencing production in June 2022.

During  this  commissioning  and  ramp-up  phase,  production  of  56,681  dmt  of  spodumene concentrate was achieved from the Ngungaju Plant for the year (previous year: nil). Ramp up to the plant's nameplate capacity of 180,000-200,000tpa of concentrate is expected during the September 2022 Quarter.

## Ore Reserves and Mineral Resources

During the year, the Company announced substantial increases in both the Mineral Resource and JORC Ore Reserve at the Pilgangoora Project following the discovery of new pegmatite domains, together with the integration of the Ngungaju Mineral Resource and Ore Reserve for the first time (refer ASX releases dated 6 September 2021 and 6 October 2021).

The updated JORC Mineral Resource represented a 39% increase in the total Measured, Indicated and Inferred Resource to 308.9 million tonnes grading 1.14% Li2O, 105ppm Ta2O5 and 0.59% Fe2O3, containing 3.5 million tonnes of Li2O and 71.7 million pounds of Ta2O5.

Based  on  this  updated  Mineral  Resource  estimate,  the  Company  announced  a  restated  and combined JORC Ore Reserve in October 2021 (inclusive of Ngungaju operations) of 161.9 Mt grading

## Exploration

Pilgangoora exploration programs during the financial year included a strategic exploration and resource extensional drilling program along the under-explored region on the tenement boundary adjacent  to  the  Ngungaju  Plant,  as  well  as  a  sterilisation  drilling  program  targeting  a  potential alternate waste dump location to the west of the Central Pit area.  Both programs returned wide and  near  surface  pegmatite  domains  which  continue  to  highlight  the  scale  and  exploration potential of the Pilgangoora project area.

Exploration at the Company's regional tenements included desk top studies, field reconnaissance and surface geochemistry programs, and small-scale drilling programs.

## Project Expansions

On 29 June 2022, the Board approved the capital investment for the ' P680 Project ' to increase the annual  production  capacity  at  Pilgan  Plant  by  100,000  dmt  through  the  introduction  of  a  new primary rejection circuit at a capital cost of $103M. This investment supports Pilbara Minerals' long term growth strategy to incrementally increase production capacity at the Pilgangoora Operation in  line  with  market  demand.  When  completed,  the  combined  annual  nameplate  production capacity  of  the  Pilgangoora  Operation  is  expected  to  be  ~640,000  to  680,000dmt  from  the December Quarter 2023.

As  part  of  the  delivery  of  the  P680  Project,  the  Company  will  replace  the  existing  contracted crushing service with a larger company-owned crushing facility designed for 5Mtpa of throughput crushing  capacity,  to  satisfy  future  expansions  needs.  Along  with  the  newly  installed  crushing facility, the Company will also introduce ore sorting technology to the front-end of the Pilgan Plant designed  to  reject  basalt  (and  other  non-lithium  hosting  materials)  earlier  in  the  production process.  The  capital  investment  for  both  the  larger  5Mtpa  crushing  facility  and  ore  sorting technology is expected to be $194M, which should deliver processing efficiencies, improved lithia recoveries and unit cost savings (including the cost saving from changing the existing contracted crushing service to a company-owned crushing facility). The new crushing and ore sorting facilities will  also  provide  flexibility  to  continue  to  expand  Pilgangoora  Operations  to  up  to  1Mtpa  of spodumene concentrate production capacity.

The P680 Project's estimated capital cost includes approximately $50M of pre-investment capital to assist  with  the  next  phased  expansion  at  the  Pilgangoora  Operation,  with  targeted  production capacity of up to 1Mtpa ( P1000 Project ). Whilst the P1000 Project remains subject to a separate final investment decision (FID), suitable market conditions, and regulatory approvals, this pre-investment of capital is designed to mitigate costs associated with retrofitting expansion capacity for both the primary rejection and crushing and ore sorting circuits at the Pilgan Plant. In addition, this preinvestment  assists  in  avoiding  future  operational  interruptions  associated  with  infrastructure duplication and brownfield interactions. FID for the P1000 Project is targeted for late December 2022.

For personal use only

## Mid-Stream Project

Following the execution of a Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) in May 2021 (refer ASX release dated 11 May 2021) with Calix Limited, the Company completed a scoping study aimed at generating a higher value and more environmentally friendly lithium product for the battery materials industry ( Mid-Stream Project ).

The scoping study, which was undertaken by Lycopodium Minerals (ASX: LYL), provides preliminary support for the technical viability of constructing a demonstration-scale chemicals facility producing value-added  lithium  phosphate  salts  via  an  innovative  refining  process  at  Pilbara  Minerals' Pilgangoora Operation.

The study indicates lithium phosphate salt as the preferred mid-stream product.  Lithium phosphate is  a  key  feedstock  for  a  number  of  battery  chemicals  manufacturers, including the  lithium ferro phosphate (LFP) battery cathode manufacturing industry, as well as lithium carbonate and lithium hydroxide production.

The Mid-Stream Project has the potential to deliver significant sustainability benefits across  the lithium supply chain via:

- substantial reduction of carbon energy requirements by the complete electrification of the Mid-Stream process, including spodumene calcining, enabling the potential to power the project using up to 100% renewables sourced power;
- rationalisation of the carbon footprint via reduced waste movement across transport and logistics  supply  chains  resulting  from  a  more  lithium-dense,  and  near  zero-waste  final product; and
- improved  lithium  recovery  from  the  ore  resource,  as  the  proposed  refining  process  is expected to have the ability to treat very fine spodumene concentrates at lower lithia grades, which  have  traditionally  been  problematic  for  calcination  in  conventional  direct-fired horizontal rotary calciners.

Following completion of the scoping study, Pilbara Minerals and Calix have agreed key commercial terms  to  progress  front  end  engineering  studies  and  for  a  joint  venture  in  an  updated  binding Memorandum of Understanding. It is expected that the joint venture will be formalised during the September Quarter 2022.  A final investment decision on the demonstration plant is targeted for early 2023.

Pilbara  Minerals  and  Calix  were  awarded  a  $20  million  grant  in  May  2022  under  the  Australian Government's  Modern  Manufacturing  Initiative  (MMI)  -  Manufacturing  Translation  Stream  to support  the  development  of  the  Mid-Stream  Project.    Funding  remains  subject  to  the  parties finalising a MMI grant funding agreement.

## POSCO Joint Venture (JV)

During the year, Pilbara Minerals formed an incorporated joint venture ( JV ) with POSCO to develop and  operate  a  43ktpa  lithium  hydroxide  monohydrate  ( LHM )  Conversion  Facility  (' Conversion Facility ') in South Korea (Refer ASX releases dated 26 October 2021 and 13 April 2022).

The Conversion Facility will consist of two production trains each with a 21.5ktpa LHM production capacity.  In conjunction with conventional purification technologies, the Conversion Facility will use POSCO's leading patented purification technology which has been developed by POSCO over the last 10 years.

The Company will initially own an 18% equity interest in the JV with the right to increase its equity interest  to  30%.  As  part  of  the  arrangement,  Pilbara  Minerals  will  supply  315ktpa  of  spodumene concentrate from the Pilgangoora Operation on commercial terms to the JV for conversion into lithium hydroxide at the jointly owned Conversion Facility. All spodumene concentrate sold under the offtake agreement will be on arms' length terms at prevailing market prices for chemical grade

14

spodumene concentrate sold on a CIF basis.

The Company's initial 18% equity participation in the JV (A$76.2M) was fully funded from the A$79.5M 5-year convertible bond provided by POSCO during the June Quarter.

Completion  of  construction  and  commencement  of  commissioning  of  the  first  train  of  the Conversion  Facility  is  expected  from  late  2023  with  completion  of  the  second  train  expected approximately 3 months later. Participation in the JV supports the Company's long-term strategy of becoming a fully integrated raw materials company with a globally diversified customer base.

## Battery Material Exchange

During the year the Company launched its Battery Material Exchange (BMX), a new digital trading and sales platform to facilitate the sale of uncommitted spodumene concentrate production.

Six  separate cargos of spodumene concentrate were sold via auction or pre-auction sale during FY2022, totalling approximately 43,000dmt sold on an SC5.5 FOB Port Hedland basis .  The Company received strong interest in all auctions, with a June 2022 cargo achieving a record pre-auction offer of  US$6,350/dmt  (SC5.5,  FOB  Port  Hedland  basis).  This  offer  equates  to  an  approximate  price  of US$7,017/dmt on a SC6.0 CIF China basis after adjusting for lithia content on a pro rata basis and freight costs.

The strong prices received for the BMX auctions provided further evidence of the robust demand for battery raw materials experienced during the year across the global lithium supply chain.

## Sustainability

During the year, Pilbara Minerals undertook a review of its sustainability strategy and its materiality topics  for  FY23  and  beyond.  This  work  included  engagement  with  both  internal  and  external stakeholders to better understand the priority areas for sustainability and integrate sustainability throughout the Company.

The Company's decarbonisation pathway was progressed to support its aim to target net zero in the decade beginning 2040. Following the development of the high-level pathway, the Company is now looking at specific projects and study work to determine the most effective and efficient ways to achieve the target and aligned with Pilbara Minerals' business strategy to sustainably grow and diversify. During the year, construction commenced on a 6MW solar array at the Pilgangoora Project, with commercial operation targeted for the December 2022 Quarter.

The Company continued to engage with its native title partners the Nyamal and Kariyarra Peoples, with meetings and heritage surveys held throughout the year to ensure both groups were actively engaged in respect of their interests. To support future works, heritage surveys were conducted with the Nyamal People to gain clearance prior to any activities being undertaken.

Work to ensure Pilbara Minerals' is fostering a safe and respectful culture and workplace continued with the 'Unmute Yourself' campaign (launched in 2021) and the roll out of Safe and Respectful Behaviours (SARB) workshops. These workshops educated employees on how to prevent sexual harassment in the workplace including outlining expected behaviours.  A total of 60 workshops were held on-site and in the Perth office, with almost 450 employees and key contractors attending the sessions. Pilbara Minerals also continued to work with industry on improving standards of behaviour and in respect of the release of the Parliamentary Report 'Enough is Enough'.

Pilbara Minerals will provide further information on its sustainability objectives and activities for the year  in  its  Sustainability  Report  which  will  be  released  as  part  of  the  Company's  FY2022  Annual Report.

## Corporate

At  30  June  2022,  the  Company  had  a  cash  balance  of  $591.7M  (30  June  2021:  $99.7M).  The  cash balance, inclusive of $282.4M of irrevocable bank letters of credit for shipments that were completed on or before 30 June 2022, was $874.2M (30 June 2021: $115.7M).

The Company appointed experienced mining and resource sector executive, Dale Henderson, as the Company's Managing Director and CEO following Ken Brinsden's resignation, with the transition being completed by 1 August 2022.  Prior to his appointment, Mr Henderson held the role of Chief Operating Officer (COO) at Pilbara Minerals since 2017.

The Company also appointed experienced resource industry executive, Miriam Stanborough, to its Board as a non-executive Director effective 4 th  October 2021. Ms Stanborough is a chemical engineer with more than 20 years' experience in the mineral processing industry across various commodities including copper, uranium, gold, silver, alumina and mineral sands.

During the year, the Company finalised agreement with its existing senior lenders to increase the Syndicated Finance Facility by US$20M to US$130M to support the restart of the Ngungaju Plant (refer ASX release dated 23 November 2021), with the funds being drawn down in December 2021. The terms and conditions of the expanded Syndicated Finance Facility, including tenure, repayment schedule, financial covenants and pricing remained substantially the same as those executed under the original existing debt facility in July 2020 (refer ASX Announcement dated 30 July 2020).  At the same time, BNP Paribas approved a US$10M increase in the Working Capital Facility to US$25M. The Working Capital Facility currently remains undrawn.

Pursuant to the terms  of  the  Syndicated  Finance  Facility,  the  Company  repaid  US$19.8M  in  the December 2021 and March 2022 Quarters under the facility's cash sweep mechanism. Subsequently the secured lenders formally waived the cash sweep mechanism for the remainder of calendar year 2022.

The  Company  also  settled  early  the  outstanding  deferred  consideration  due  to  the  Altura  loan noteholders as part of the acquisition of Altura Lithium Operations Pty Ltd by way of shares. Prior to settlement, the Company executed a variation deed to reduce the number of shares to be paid in satisfaction of the early repayment of the deferred consideration by ~5%. The deferred consideration was settled by way of two equal tranches of 32.67 million shares each, on 17 August 2021 and 8 October 2021. Following final settlement, a deferred consideration fair value movement expense of $37.2M was recorded to recognise the profit and loss impact of the increase in the Company's share price between 30 June 2021 and the dates of final settlement.
